## Review

**8 out of 10, Strong.** Presentation quality, judged with the SlideDeck Co review heuristic (https://slidedeck.co/review-methodology/). The ask, the evidence and the merit of the idea are not rated.

The deck has a clear three-part route (future, present, past) set up on slide 2 and signposted by dark divider slides at 3, 35 and 49. Each content slide has a short title with a subtitle that carries the point, and the sourced charts (11, 26, 38, 54, 62) are cleanly drawn with red used to mark the series that matters. The restrained red-on-grey identity, consistent footer and mix of diagrams, charts and product screenshots keep the rhythm steady across a long deck. Weaknesses are small text in subtitles, footers and chart axes, sparse or empty divider and quote slides, and some repetitive three-circle diagrams that add little beyond a bullet list.

**Worth keeping:** Keep the title-plus-argument-subtitle pattern, the red highlight on the key series in charts, and the two-column before/after layouts such as slide 5.

**Most useful repair:** Enlarge subtitle, axis, legend and source text, and put the legends closer to the data on slides 11, 24 and 42.

**What would change the score:** Replace the repeated three-circle diagrams (slides 13, 18, 21, 30, 48) with more varied or more informative visuals, and trim the near-empty divider slides.

_Confidence medium. 72 of 94 pages inspected on contact sheets, 4 at readable size. Reviewed Reviewed 2026-11-08. Model-assisted review (claude-sonnet-5-5)._

## Summary

The deck opens with a title slide and a three-part framing: future, present and past, shown as three circles. It then moves through the future section on tech visions for 2030, covering the Web3 and metaverse narratives, and tests them against market data and history. The present section looks at what startups and enterprises are deploying, with a focus on cloud, SaaS and workflow software. The past section traces how the internet has disrupted older industries, using music, television, retail and newspapers as cases. The closing section ties the three parts together and reflects on the scale of tech investment. Slides worth studying include the 'great rebranding' slide, which uses a simple two-column layout to recast crypto and VR, and the Web3 evolution diagram on slide 8, which places three stages on one arrow. The deck relies heavily on sourced line and bar charts with short headline titles and a subtitle that carries the argument, and it uses a restrained palette of red accents on grey.

## Questions about this deck

**How many slides does the Benedict Evans presentation deck have, and how is it structured?**

The deck has 94 slides. Slide 2 divides it into three parts shown as circles: Future (tech visions for 2030), Present (startups deploying the ideas of the 2010s) and Past (the old economy disrupted by ideas from the 2000s).

**What does the Benedict Evans deck say about how far the move to cloud has gone?**

Slide 38, titled 'The move to cloud is still just beginning', states that only 10-15% of enterprise IT spending has moved to the cloud so far. The same slide's subtitle adds a figure of 20-30% for workflows.

**What does the Benedict Evans deck say about VR traction so far?**

Slide 23 says there is growing interest and that Oculus Quest 2 sold 10m units in 12 months, but it did not surge in lockdown. The slide's source is Google Trends.

**What does the Benedict Evans deck say about Web3 compared with Web 2.0?**

Slide 8, titled 'Web3 - a dream of a next version of the internet', places Web 2.0, where users read and publishers create and make money, beside Web3, where users create and users control networks and money. The slide states that a blockchain is a distributed, open computing system, or at least that is the plan.

### Slide 5

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: A clean two-column comparison with arrows shows how the same underlying technologies are being relabelled, using minimal text and a single accent colour.
- Worth keeping: Keep the title-plus-argument-subtitle pattern, the red highlight on the key series in charts, and the two-column before/after layouts such as slide 5.

**Text**

The great rebranding
Redefine the vision and set a broader goal, for both VR and crypto
Cryptocurrencies
Store of value, payments, / money transfer (and / speculation)
Web3
New models for building / software, internet businesses / and networks
VR/AR
Gaming headsets, niche / industrial tool, science projects
Metaverse
The next platform after / smartphones
Benedict Evans — January 2022
5

**Layout**

Two-row, two-column arrangement on a 12-column style grid with left text column starting at about 20% of slide width and right column at about 73%. The title sits top-left at about 5.5% from the top, with a subtitle beneath it, both left-aligned to the same left edge. A white middle band holds two horizontal rows, each with a left heading and body, a red dotted arrow in the centre, and a right heading and body; a thin light-grey gap separates the two rows. The footer, left-aligned in a grey strip at the bottom, reads Benedict Evans, a dash and January 2022, with the slide number at the far right at about 95% of width and about 91% from the top.

**Visuals**

Two red dotted horizontal arrows, each with a solid triangular arrowhead pointing right. Arrow 1 runs from about 49.4% to 65.0% of slide width at about 36% of slide height, connecting the Cryptocurrencies block on the left to the Web3 block on the right. Arrow 2 runs from about 49.4% to 65.0% of slide width at about 70% of slide height, connecting the VR/AR block to the Metaverse block. Dotted line of small evenly spaced red dashes, approximately 0.6% of slide height thick, colour about #E84A3F. No photographs, charts, logos or icons.

**Design**

Background is a flat light grey #F1F1F1 for the top title band and bottom footer band, with a pure white #FFFFFF middle band and a thin #F2F2F2 separator strip between the two content rows. Title and subtitle and all headings and body text are dark grey #545454. Accent arrows are red #E84A3F. Footer text is light grey #A6A6A6. Typefaces: title, subtitle, headings and body are a high-contrast serif in the PhotinaMT family (headings in PhotinaMT SemiBold, body in regular); footer is HelveticaNeue sans-serif, bold for the name and regular for the date and number. Title is about 5.6% of slide height, subtitle about 2.6%, headings about 3.3%, body about 3.0%, footer about 1.9%. Shapes are plain rectangles with no corner radius, shadow or gradient.

### Slide 8

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: Three circles joined by arrows show the evolution from Web to Web 2.0 to Web3 in a simple, legible diagram that carries the argument without extra text.

**Text**

Web3 - a dream of a next version of the internet
A blockchain is a distributed, open computing system - or at least, that’s the plan
Web
Users read
Publishers create
and make money
Web 2.0
Users create
Networks control &
make money
Web3
Users create
Users control
networks & money
Benedict Evans
–– January 2022
8

**Layout**

Single-column composition on a light grey field. The title sits top-left at about 20% from the left edge, with a one-line subtitle directly beneath it, both left-aligned to the same edge. Three equal white circles sit in a horizontal row across the middle of the slide (about 34% to 73% of the slide height), spaced evenly with two grey arrows between them. Footer text is right-aligned in the lower right at about 91% of the height, with the page number at the far right edge, roughly 95% across.

**Visuals**

Three white circles of equal size (about 21.7% of slide width each) with thin dark grey outlines, connected left to right by two solid grey block arrows. Circle 1 (left) and circle 2 (centre) have dark grey #545454 outlines of about 0.3% of slide height; circle 3 (right) has a red #E74C3C outline of the same weight, highlighting it. Each circle holds three stacked centred text lines: a bold heading, then two body lines. Left circle: Web / Users read / Publishers create and make money. Centre circle: Web 2.0 / Users create / Networks control & make money. Right circle: Web3 / Users create / Users control networks & money. Arrow 1 is a block arrow with a rectangular shaft and triangular head pointing right, fill #A7A7A7, between circles 1 and 2. Arrow 2 is identical, between circles 2 and 3. No photographs, logos or charts.

**Design**

Background: Solid flat fill #F1F1F1 (light grey) covering the entire slide. Solid light grey background #F1F1F1 with no gradient or texture. Title and subtitle in dark grey #545454. Circle headings in semibold serif #545454; circle body text in regular serif #545454. Footer text in mid grey #A6A6A6. Accent: red #E74C3C outline on the Web3 circle only. Typefaces: title and body in Photina (a book serif, roman, not italic); circle headings in Photina SemiBold; footer in Helvetica Neue (sans-serif), with Bold for the name and regular for the date and page number. Title is about 5.6% of slide height, subtitle about 2.6%, circle headings about 3.3%, circle body about 3.0%, footer about 1.9%. Circles have plain flat fills, no shadows, no rounded corners beyond the circle geometry. Arrows are flat with no outline.

### Slide 9

**Text**

Web3 = Open Source 2 / Open source changed how code is written - Web3 tries to change where it runs / Open Source / Open, distributed code creation / No direct revenue / Web & SaaS / Mostly powered by Open Source / But controlled, run & sold centrally / Web3 / Open code / Open & distributed execution / Integrated revenue / Benedict Evans — January 2022 / 9

**Layout**

Left-aligned title and subtitle sit in the upper left, starting at about 20% of slide width and 5.5% of height, with the title above a one-line subtitle. Three equal white circles sit in a single horizontal row at the vertical centre, connected by two grey block arrows, leaving wide empty margins left and right. The footer 'Benedict Evans — January 2022' and slide number '9' sit on one baseline at the bottom right, around 91% of height.

**Visuals**

Three white circles of equal size in a horizontal row, each with a text stack inside, joined left to right by two solid light-grey block arrows. Circle 1 (Open Source) has a solid dark grey outline. Circle 2 (Web & SaaS) has a dotted dark grey outline. Circle 3 (Web3) has a solid red outline. Arrow 1 sits between circles 1 and 2 and arrow 2 sits between circles 2 and 3; both point right. No photographs, logos or charts.

**Design**

Background: Solid flat light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide. Flat light-grey background with no gradient or texture. Title and body text in dark grey #545454 in a Photina-style serif (Monotype Photina or close serif, regular weight), with headings in Photina SemiBold. Title is about 5.6% of slide height; subtitle and body text about 2.6% to 3.0%; circle headings about 3.3%. Footer is HelveticaNeue in light grey #A6A6A6, the name in bold. Circle outlines are about 0.3% of height thick (dark grey solid, dark grey dotted, red #E74C3C solid); circle fills are white #FFFFFF. Arrows are flat grey #A7A7A7 block arrows with no shadow. No corner radii, shadows or gradients.

### Slide 10

**Text**

A messianic vision for tech
Like open source and the internet in their early days, a tech movement with a strong dose of religion
Closed and centralised
Open and decentralised
Companies make money
Users make money
All the old problems
All the old problems, in
exciting new ways
Benedict Evans
–– January 2022
10

**Layout**

Full-width horizontal band layout with a light grey header zone containing the title and subtitle, followed by three stacked white rows separated by thin grey gutters, each row holding a left-aligned phrase, a centred red dotted arrow, and a right-aligned phrase. The title and subtitle are left-aligned at about 20% of the slide width, and the footer line (name, date, page number) sits right of centre at about 91% of the slide height, with the page number at the far right.

**Visuals**

Three red dotted arrows, one in each white row, horizontally oriented, pointing right from the left phrase toward the right phrase. Each arrow runs from about x 49.4% to 65.0% of slide width, with a small solid triangular arrowhead at the right end. Arrow colour is about #E8474A (red), with a dot pattern of small evenly spaced round dots, approximately 0.5% of slide height in diameter. No photographs, charts, logos or icons.

**Design**

Background is light grey #F1F1F1 across the full slide with white #FFFFFF bands for the three content rows and thin #F2F2F2 gutters between them. Title and subtitle are set in a serif face resembling PhotinaMT (a Photina-style transitional serif), regular weight, colour #545454. Title is about 5.6% of slide height; subtitle and row phrases are about 2.6% and 3.3% of slide height respectively. Row phrases are regular serif #545454. Footer uses a Helvetica Neue sans-serif, with the name in bold and the date and page number in regular, colour #A6A6A6, about 1.9% of slide height. Accent colour for the arrows is red #E8474A. Shapes are plain rectangles with square corners, no shadows, no gradients.

### Slide 11

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: A single well-labelled time-series chart with a clear title, subtitle and source line makes the market data readable at a glance.
- Suggested repair: Enlarge subtitle, axis, legend and source text, and put the legends closer to the data on slides 11, 24 and 42.

**Text**

Obligatory market cap chart / Speculation and lots of market mechanics - but also billions of dollars of real money invested / Market capitalisations for the two largest crypto networks ($bn) / Bitcoin / Ethereum / 1,250 / 1,000 / 750 / 500 / 250 / 0 / 2017 / 2018 / 2019 / 2020 / 2021 / Source: CoinMarketCap / Benedict Evans / –– January 2022 / 11

**Layout**

Full-width light grey header band (top about 20.5% of the slide) holds the title and subtitle left-aligned at about x 20%, with a white content band below containing the chart; a light grey footer band holds the source line at left (x 20.5%) and the author, date and page number at right, ending near x 95%. The chart legend sits at the far left (x 4.8%) above the chart plot area, which spans roughly x 17% to 95% and y 35% to 80%.

**Visuals**

Line chart titled 'Market capitalisations for the two largest crypto networks ($bn)'. Y axis: market capitalisation in $bn, range 0 to 1,250 with gridlines at 250, 500, 750, 1,000 and 1,250 (dotted light grey horizontal lines). X axis: years 2017, 2018, 2019, 2020, 2021 with the time series running from 2017 to late 2021/early 2022. Series 1 Bitcoin: dark grey (#545454) line; about 15 in early 2017, about 330 near late 2017 peak around early 2018 (about 320 peak), about 60-150 through 2018-2020, rising to about 1,250 peak at the far right with about 1,100 at the end, peaking near 1,200 mid-2021 and a dip to about 550 in mid-late 2021. Series 2 Ethereum: red (#E84C3D-like) line; near 0 in 2017, about 130 peak early 2018, about 30 through 2019, about 50 in 2020, about 480 peak mid-2021, about 570 peak late 2021 and about 500 at the end. Legend at left: dark grey line marker labelled Bitcoin, red line marker labelled Ethereum. No individual points are highlighted. Chart sits on white.

**Design**

Background: Light grey #F1F1F1 overall, with a white band #FFFFFF behind the chart from y 20.5% to 86%.

### Slide 14

**Text**

NFTs, today’s white-hot idea
Explosion of interest and quoted value
‘Cultural value’ versus ‘intrinsic value’ is not a problematic / concept - ask Supreme
But tiny absolute numbers of people so far - by design! - and / lots of noise and circularity
Interesting as experiments in digital property…
But also a tech building block with broader use cases
Source: OpenSea
Benedict Evans — January 2022
14

**Layout**

Two-column split at the slide midpoint: a light grey left panel (about 50% width) holds the title, a bulleted-style list of five paragraphs and the source line; a white right panel holds a single browser-window screenshot centred vertically, with the footer and page number along the bottom. Title sits top-left of the left column at about 24% height, left margin about 4.6%; the footer 'Benedict Evans — January 2022' and page number '14' sit at about 91% height, right-aligned to the right edge of the slide.

**Visuals**

One photograph-style screenshot of a web browser window showing the OpenSea 'Top NFTs' page, placed at x 58.1%, y 13.1%, w 34.8%, h 73.8% (669 by 797 px). Browser chrome is light grey (#E8E8E8) with three grey traffic-light dots at top left and a rounded address bar reading 'opensea.io' with a padlock and reload icon. Below: OpenSea logo (blue circle #2081E2 with white sail mark, wordmark 'OpenSea' in bold black) at left, a rounded search field with magnifier icon and placeholder 'Search items, collections, and accounts', and a hamburger menu icon at right. Heading 'Top NFTs' in large bold black sans-serif, centred; subtitle 'The top NFTs on OpenSea, ranked by volume, floor price and other statistics.' in grey. Three white rounded filter dropdowns with grey borders: 'Last 7 days', 'Art' with a blue icon, 'All chains' with a link icon, each with a chevron. Ranked list row 1: '1' then circular avatar of Bored Ape Yacht Club, bold name 'Bored Ape Yacht Club', subtext '- Less', price '7,377.98' with ETH diamond icon and '-3.54%' in red; stats row with labels '7d %' value '-70.58%' (red), 'Floor Price' value '44.89' with ETH icon, 'Owners' value '5.9K', 'Items' value '10.0K'. Row 2: '2' then circular avatar of CryptoPunks (teal background with pixel character), bold name 'CryptoPunks', subtext '+ More', price '6,482.77' with ETH icon and '+100.79%' in green. Footer pagination: left button '< 1 - 100' greyed, right button '101 - 201 >' outlined. Screenshot has a soft drop shadow and no crop; it is shown fully, boxed within the white panel. Lighting is flat, even, and dominant colours are white, light grey, black, and blue #2081E2 accent with red #E5484D and green #00C46A figures.

**Design**

Background: Split background: left half solid light grey #F2F2F2, right half solid white #FFFFFF. Clean minimal editorial look. ### Slide 15

**Text**

New incentive structures
Helium is a distributed network of low power, low
bandwidth, IoT radio access points
Decentralised incentives, tracking and payment
Source: Helium
Benedict Evans
–– January 2022
15

**Layout**

Two-column split slide: the left half (about 47% width) holds a text block on a light grey field with the title at upper-left, the body text below it and the source line near the bottom-left; the right half is a full-bleed map photograph running top to bottom edge to edge. Left margin about 4.6% of slide width; footer row at about 91% of slide height across the left and bottom edge, with author name, date and page number in a single line at the bottom of the right-hand white area.

**Visuals**

Full-bleed dark map of the San Francisco Bay Area (right half, x 47.1 to 100, full height) in a dark slate-blue street-map style with place labels in small pale text including Novato, Vallejo, Antioch, Concord, Richmond, San Ramon, Pleasanton, San Francisco, South San Francisco, Princeton-by-the-Sea, Fremont, Milpitas-area label Stanford (partly visible as 'Stanford'), Lobitos, San Jose and Woodville. Overlaid are hexagonal coverage cells clustered along the bay shoreline and corridors: green hexagons indicate sparser coverage in the north and east, and orange-to-red hexagons indicate denser coverage concentrated around San Francisco, the East Bay corridor and the peninsula down to San Jose. The map is not cropped in a box; it is full-bleed to the right and top and bottom edges. Dominant colours: dark navy-slate #1E2D4A base with green #00B050-like and orange-red #D9703A-like hexagons. No chart axes or legend are printed.

**Design**

Background: Light grey #F2F2F2 on the left, with the right half given to a full-bleed dark map photograph. Minimal editorial look. Background of left half is light grey #F2F2F2; the right image occupies the area over a white #FFFFFF panel. Title in a serif typeface resembling Photina (regular weight, mid-grey) in #545454, large, roughly 5.6% of slide height. Body text in the same serif at about 3.0% of slide height, #545454, regular weight, with a blank line between the two body paragraphs. Source line and footer in a neo-grotesque sans-serif resembling Helvetica Neue at about 1.85% of slide height; source line #545454, author name in bold #A6A6A6, date and page number regular #A6A6A6. No rules, shapes, shadows or corner radii.

**Layout**

Full-width light grey header band across the top holding the left-aligned title and subtitle, with a white content band below containing a single line chart with its title above the plot area, and a light grey footer band at the bottom holding the source line at left and the author, date and slide number at right. **Visuals**

Line chart titled 'Active Helium hotspots'. Type: single smoothed line series in red (about #E8453C), stroke about 2 px wide, no markers. Y-axis: number of active hotspots, range 0 to 350,000, labelled gridlines at 0, 50,000, 100,000, 150,000, 200,000, 250,000, 300,000, 350,000; light grey dotted horizontal gridlines (about #BFBFBF) at each labelled value. X-axis: time, labelled at September 2019, March 2020, September 2020, March 2021, September 2021; thin grey baseline at 0. Series shape: starts near 0 in September 2019, rises slowly to roughly 5,000 by late 2020 (about), climbs to roughly 20,000 by March 2021 (about), then rises steeply to about 320,000 at the right end near September 2021 to January 2022 (about). No legend; no highlighted points; no data labels.

### Slide 17

**Text**

What are we looking at?
Massive noise, hype and irrational criticism from all directions
Market caps?
A share price is an opinion of the future; / operating metrics are data about the / present
A token price is neither, and both
So - is speculation validation?
Ponzi schemes?
Currencies, art and social networks are / based on collective adoption
And early adopters benefit most…
But this is also true of Ponzi schemes
Benedict Evans –– January 2022
17

**Layout**

Full-width light grey header band (top 0 to about 20.5% of height) holds the title and subtitle left-aligned at x 20.1%, aligned to a content margin. Below it, a white body panel spans about 20.5% to 86% of height with two text columns, left column at x 20.1% and right column at x 64.0%, separated by a vertical dotted red divider at about x 62.9%, spanning roughly y 31% to 78%. A light grey footer band sits at the bottom from about 86% down, with the author name, date and slide number right-aligned near the right edge at y about 91%.

**Visuals**

A vertical dotted red line (kind of line, stroke about #E8443A, dotted with small square dots, roughly 0.3% of slide width) at x about 62.9%, running from y about 31.0% to y about 78.0%, dividing the two text columns. No photographs, charts, logos or icons.

**Design**

Background is a two-tone flat scheme: light grey #F1F1F1 for the header and footer bands and white #FFFFFF for the body panel. Title text is grey #545454 in PhotinaMT regular serif at about 5.6% of slide height. Subtitle is grey #545454 in PhotinaMT regular serif at about 2.6% of slide height. Column headings are dark grey #474747 in PhotinaMT SemiBold serif at about 3.4% of slide height. Body text is dark grey #474747 in PhotinaMT regular serif at about 3.15% of slide height with generous paragraph spacing. Footer text is light grey #A6A6A6 in Helvetica Neue sans-serif at about 1.85% of slide height, with the author name bold. Accent is the red dotted divider about #E8443A. Line and shape styles: flat rectangles with no corner radius, no shadows and no gradients; the divider is a dotted line.

### Slide 18

**Review notes**

- What would change the score: Replace the repeated three-circle diagrams (slides 13, 18, 21, 30, 48) with more varied or more informative visuals, and trim the near-empty divider slides.

**Text**

Early (still!), exciting, unclear
Looks much like the early consumer internet, or open source (but also like lots of other things that never worked)
Deep technical / understanding / still needed
Fragmented & / complex
Massive creative / energy, but also / huge uncertainty
Benedict Evans —— January 2022
18

**Layout**

Left-aligned title block starts at about x 20% at the top, with a one-line subtitle beneath it; three equal circles sit in a horizontal row across the middle-lower area, evenly spaced, with the left circle at x 19.2% and the right circle ending near x 96%. The footer sits bottom-right: author name and date at about x 75% to 91%, and the page number at x 94.4%, all on one baseline near y 91%. No slide-number margin on the left; the content is not aligned to a left margin at x 0.

**Visuals**

Three filled circles of equal size (about 22.1% of slide width by 39.3% of slide height, rendered as perfect circles). Left circle: dark grey #535353, text 'Deep technical understanding still needed'. Middle circle: dark grey #535353, text 'Fragmented & complex'. Right circle: red-coral #EB483F, text 'Massive creative energy, but also huge uncertainty'. Flat fills, no outline, no shadow, no gradient. No photographs, charts or icons.

**Design**

Background flat light grey #F1F1F1 with no texture. Title and subtitle text in grey #545454. Body text inside circles in white #FFFFFF. Footer author name in bold grey #A6A6A6, date and page number in regular grey #A6A6A6. Accent colour is red #EB483F used on the right circle only. Typefaces: title and subtitle and circle text are a serif (PhotinaMT, a Photina-style serif with regular weight); footer is a neo-grotesque sans-serif (Helvetica Neue, bold for the name, regular for the date and number). Title size about 5.6% of slide height, subtitle about 2.6%, circle text about 3.3%, footer about 1.9%. Circles have no corner radius because they are circles; no shadows.

### Slide 19

**Text**

Is an iPhone open or closed? / Yes
The iPhone is full of open source software… / And yet it’s closed… / And yet there are millions of apps and billions of / app installs / So what do open or decentralised mean? Where, / and how, at what layers?
Source: Apple
Benedict Evans
–– January 2022
19

**Layout**

Two equal columns split at the vertical midline: a light grey left panel holds the title, body text and source line, and a white right panel holds a boxed product photograph. The title sits top-left of the text block at about 24% of slide height, with the body paragraphs left-aligned below it at a shared left margin of about 4.6% of slide width. The footer runs along the bottom at about 91% height: the source line at the left margin, and the author, date and slide number right-aligned in the white column.

**Visuals**

One photograph, not a chart or table. It shows a first-generation iPhone, front and back, on a plain white background. The front face is angled toward the viewer, showing a black bezel, a glossy black screen with a status bar reading AT&T, signal and wifi icons, 9:42 AM and a battery icon. The home screen shows a 4-by-3 grid of app icons: Text, Calendar (Tuesday 9), Photos, Camera; YouTube, Stocks, Maps, Weather (73°); Clock, Calculator, Notes, Settings. A dock along the bottom of the screen holds Phone, Mail, Safari and iPod. A round black home button sits below the screen. The back of the device is visible to the right in brushed silver with a black lower band and a white Apple logo near the centre-right. Lighting is soft and even, studio style, with subtle highlights on the metal edges. Dominant colours are silver grey, black and white, with coloured app icons (green, blue, orange, yellow). The image is not cropped, is not full-bleed, and is placed as a boxed rectangle with no border or overlay, framed in the right half of the slide.

**Design**

Background: Split background: #F2F2F2 on the left half and #FFFFFF on the right half. Split background: light grey panel on the left half and white on the right half, with no gradients, shadows or pattern. Title and body text are dark grey #545454 in a regular-weight Photina MT serif (Times-like, serif). Title is about 5.6% of slide height and body text about 3.5% of slide height, with the title set in one size larger than the body. Body paragraphs are separated by generous spacing. Footer text is set in Helvetica Neue sans-serif at about 1.9% of slide height: source line in dark grey #545454, author name in Helvetica Neue Bold #A6A6A6, date and page number in regular #A6A6A6. No accent colour, rules, rounded corners or shapes are used beyond the two panel fills.

23

**Layout**

Full-width three-band slide: a light grey top band holds the title and subtitle, a white middle band (y 20.5 to 86.1 percent) holds the chart, and a light grey footer band holds the source line, the author and date, and the page number. Title and subtitle are left-aligned on a common left edge at x 20.1 percent, with the chart legend at the left margin (x 4.8 percent) and the chart block starting at x about 18 percent. The source line sits at x 20.5 percent, aligned with the chart title and plot edge. The page number is right-aligned at the right margin (ends about 95.6 percent) in the footer.

**Visuals**

One line chart, titled Relative search interest, USA. Type: time-series line chart with two series, VR (light grey) and Oculus (red). Y axis: relative search interest, unitless index 0 to 100, labelled 0, 25, 50, 75, 100, with dotted horizontal gridlines at each of those values. X axis: time, labelled 2010, 2015 and 2020, with a solid grey baseline at 0; the series run from 2010 to about early 2022, monthly points. Legend at the left of the chart: a short light grey line segment labelled VR above a short red line segment labelled Oculus. VR series values (estimates, no values printed): about 5 to 6 from 2010 to 2012, about 4 in 2012 to 2013, about 3 to 4 in 2014, rising to about 15 to 20 in 2016, a peak of about 100 around early 2017 (highlighted as the chart maximum), about 35 to 50 in 2017 to 2018, a secondary peak of about 62 in late 2017, about 49 in 2019, about 49 in 2020, about 68 is not VR; VR peaks about 48 to 50 in early 2021, and ends about 40 in early 2022. Oculus series values (estimates): about 2 at its start in late 2012, about 5 to 7 in 2013 to 2014, a spike of about 35 in 2014, about 10 to 17 in 2015, about 8 to 15 in 2016 to 2018, about 43 in late 2019, about 20 to 17 in 2020, a peak of about 68 in early 2021 (highlighted as the highest Oculus point), about 33 in mid 2021, and about 38 at the end of the series in early 2022. Series colours: VR light grey, Oculus red #E8402A. Lines are about 2 px thick with no markers.

**Design**

Background: Light grey #F1F1F1 full-slide background, with a white #FFFFFF band from y 20.5 to 86.1 percent behind the chart. Clean, minimal, flat look. Background is light grey top and footer bands with a white chart band. Title is a regular-weight high-contrast serif in dark grey, subtitle is a regular serif in dark grey, and chart labels and footer are a neo-grotesque sans-serif (Helvetica Neue style) in mid grey and dark grey. Title size about 5.6 percent of slide height, subtitle about 2.6 percent, chart title about 2.2 percent bold, axis and legend text about 1.85 percent. Accent colour is the red Oculus series, red #E8402A; the VR series is light grey #D9D9D9. Gridlines are thin dotted grey #B0B0B0. No shadows, gradients or rounded shapes.

**Layout**

Full-bleed light grey slide divided into three horizontal bands: a grey header band at the top holding the title and subtitle, a white central band holding the line chart, and a grey footer band. The title and subtitle are left-aligned at x 20.1%, and the chart and source line share a left edge near x 20.5%. The chart legend sits in the left margin at x 4.8%. The footer carries the source line at left and the author, date and slide number at right, with the page number at x 94.4%.

**Visuals**

Line chart titled 'Relative search interest, USA'. Y axis: relative search interest, unitless index from 0 to 100, with dotted horizontal gridlines and labels at 0, 25, 50, 75 and 100. X axis: time from 2010 to about 2022, labelled 2010, 2015 and 2020, with a solid grey baseline at 0. Three series, with no printed values, so values are estimates. VR (light grey line): about 2 in 2010, about 1 to 3 through 2014, a spike to about 40 near 2017, then about 10 to 25 through 2018 to 2022, ending about 15. Oculus (red line): starts about 1 in late 2012, a spike to about 14 near 2014.5, mostly 2 to 8 from 2015 to 2019, rising to about 16 near 2020, peaking about 27 near 2021, ending about 15. Roblox (dark grey line): about 5 in 2010, rising slowly to about 15 by 2014, about 30 to 45 in 2016 to 2018, about 35 to 45 in 2019, a sharp rise to about 100 near 2020.5, then about 60 to 90 through 2021 to 2022, ending about 68. Legend: three entries stacked vertically at upper left of the chart, each with a short horizontal line swatch: VR light grey #D9D9D9, Oculus red #E8453C, Roblox dark grey #4D4D4D. No highlighted points are marked. Line weight is about 2 px, with no markers.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide, with a white #FFFFFF band from 20.5% to 86.1% of the height.

25

**Layout**

Three equal white circles sit in a single horizontal row across the centre of the slide, joined by two grey block arrows, with the title and subtitle left-aligned at the top and a footer line at bottom right. The title starts at about 20% from the left edge, and the slide number sits at the far right margin.

**Visuals**

Three white circles with thin outlines, each about 21.7% of slide width and 38.5% of slide height, with the same vertical centre. The first and second circles have dark grey outlines (#545454) and the third has a red outline (about #E74C3C). Between circles one and two, and between two and three, sits a solid right-pointing block arrow in #A7A7A7 (about 5.2% wide and 9.3% high) with a rectangular shaft and triangular head. Each circle holds a bold heading above a regular body text block, centred. No photographs, logos or charts.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide. Flat light grey background (#F1F1F1) with no gradient or texture. Title and subtitle are in a serif typeface (PhotinaMT, a Photina-style serif, regular weight) in dark grey #545454; circle headings are PhotinaMT SemiBold in #545454; body text in circles is PhotinaMT regular in #545454. Title is about 5.6% of slide height, subtitle and body text about 2.6% to 3.0%. Footer is HelveticaNeue sans-serif in #A6A6A6, the name in bold, at about 1.9% of slide height. Circles have white fills, stroke widths roughly 0.3% of slide height, no shadows. Arrows are flat with no outline. Accent colour is the red outline of the third circle.

### Slide 26

**Text**

Games are still relatively small / There are probably 250-350m active ‘core gamers’ - the same size as Snap and a fraction of the 1bn+ iPhone users / Global daily active users*, 2021 (m) / 350 / 300 / 250 / 200 / 150 / 100 / 50 / 0 / XBox & PS MAU / Steam / Oculus Quest 2 / unit sales / Free Fire / Honor of Kings / Roblox / Fortnite / Snap / Source: Companies / * Except Consoles and Oculus, as stated / Benedict Evans / –– January 2022 / 26

**Layout**

Full-bleed light grey slide with a white central panel. The title sits top-left at the left margin (x 20.1%), with a one-line subtitle directly beneath it, both left-aligned on a shared left edge. The chart occupies the white panel with its y-axis labels at x 18% and the plot running to about x 95%. A footer row at the bottom holds the source note left-aligned at x 20.5%, the author, date and page number right-aligned at about x 95%.

**Visuals**

Vertical bar chart titled 'Global daily active users*, 2021 (m)'. Y-axis: 'm' users, scale 0 to 350 with labelled ticks every 50 (0, 50, 100, 150, 200, 250, 300, 350), faint dotted horizontal gridlines at each tick. X-axis: categorical, labelled XBox & PS MAU, Steam, Oculus Quest 2 unit sales, Free Fire, Honor of Kings, Roblox, Fortnite, Snap; solid grey baseline at 0. Single series, eight bars, values about: XBox & PS MAU about 210 (dark grey #535353), Steam about 63 (dark grey #535353), Oculus Quest 2 unit sales about 10 (dark grey #535353), Free Fire about 150 (mid grey #A7A7A7), Honor of Kings about 100 (mid grey #A7A7A7), Roblox about 43 (mid grey #A7A7A7), Fortnite about 25 (mid grey #A7A7A7), Snap about 293 (red #EB483F, the only highlighted point). No legend. Bars are flat-filled rectangles with no outline, no shadow, equal width of about 3.8% of slide width, evenly spaced.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide, with a white (#FFFFFF) panel from y 20.5% to 86.1% full width. Light grey background #F1F1F1 with a white central band. Titles in grey #545454, body and axis text in dark grey #545554, footer author and page number in light grey #A6A6A6. Accent: red #EB483F for the Snap bar; dark grey #535353 and mid grey #A7A7A7 for the other bars. Title typeface is a serif similar to Photina (regular weight), about 5.6% of slide height; subtitle is the same serif at about 2.6%. Chart labels and footer are Helvetica Neue sans-serif (regular, with bold for the chart heading and author name) at about 1.9%; chart heading is Helvetica Neue Bold at about 2.2%. Shapes have square corners, no shadows, no gradients; gridlines are thin dotted grey.

**Layout**

A full-width grey header band holds the title and subtitle, left-aligned at about 20% of slide width. A white content area carries a single stacked column chart with its chart title and a legend at the left edge. A grey footer band holds the source line at left and the author, date and page number at right. The page number sits at the far right margin, about 94% across.

**Visuals**

Stacked column chart, titled 'Global games revenue estimates ($bn)'. Y-axis: $bn, 0 to 200, labelled at 0, 50, 100, 150, 200, with light dotted horizontal gridlines at each label. X-axis: years 2012 to 2020, one column per year. Three stacked series from bottom to top: Console (dark grey #545454), PC (light grey #A6A6A6), Mobile (red #EB483F). Values are not printed on the slide; estimates: Console about 32, 30, 30, 30, 32, 33, 34, 36, 38; PC top about 58, 59, 60, 61, 64, 66, 68, 70, 73; Mobile top about 71, 76, 84, 93, 106, 122, 138, 152, 167 (in $bn, cumulative). Legend at upper left of chart, listed top to bottom: Mobile (red square), PC (grey square), Console (dark grey square). No highlighted points. Columns are about 3.3% of slide width with even spacing; no data labels.

**Design**

Background: Light grey #F1F1F1 full-slide fill, with a white #FFFFFF band behind the chart area. Light grey #F1F1F1 header and footer bands framing a white #FFFFFF content area. Title in a serif display face resembling Photina (regular weight, about 5.6% of slide height) in dark grey #545454. Subtitle in the same serif, about 2.6% of slide height, #545454. Chart title and axis labels in a sans-serif resembling Helvetica Neue, chart title bold, about 1.9 to 2.2% of slide height, #545454. Source line in Helvetica Neue regular, #545454. Author name in Helvetica Neue bold #A6A6A6; date and page number in Helvetica Neue regular #A6A6A6. Accent colour red #EB483F used only for the Mobile series. Bars are flat with no shadows, gradients, or rounded corners; thin white separators between stacked segments. Gridlines are thin dotted grey.

### Slide 28

**Text**

How universal do VR & AR become?
Moore’s Law took ‘mobile internet’ to everyone - but only took games consoles to a 200m unit installed base
The technology can
always get better…
But that doesn’t
guarantee universal
appeal
Benedict Evans
—— January 2022
28

**Layout**

Full-width grey header band across the top holding the title and subtitle, left-aligned at about 20% from the left edge. Below it, two white horizontal bands stacked with a thin grey gap; each band has a left object, a red dotted arrow, a right object, and a text statement at about 71% from the left. Footer line at the bottom right with author, date and slide number on one baseline.

**Visuals**

Four photographs in two rows, each pair linked by a red dotted arrow. Row 1: left, a Nokia-style candy-bar mobile phone, olive-grey body with a black keypad and silver face, angled about 20 degrees, standing upright; right, an early iPhone shown front and back, front with black bezel and a grid of app icons, back in silver and black, facing right. Row 2: left, a Nintendo Entertainment System console, light grey top with dark grey and black trim and two grey controllers with red buttons in front; right, a PlayStation 5 console standing upright, white body with a black stripe and a blue light edge. Arrows: four red dotted lines with solid triangular arrowheads pointing right, each spanning from the left object to the right object at the object's mid-height, about 1.2 times the arrow thickness in red #E0443A. Photographs are unboxed, on white and grey, with no overlay or tint and no cropping.

**Design**

Background: Light grey #F1F1F1 full-slide background, with white #FFFFFF bands behind the two object rows.

### Slide 30

**Review notes**

- What would change the score: Replace the repeated three-circle diagrams (slides 13, 18, 21, 30, 48) with more varied or more informative visuals, and trim the near-empty divider slides.

**Text**

“This is amazing - it must be part of the future”
Tech determinism - for both VR and ‘crypto’ the tech is great, so it must be useful, but how?
Internet, web, / open source, / smartphones
But also drones, / 3D printing, / games consoles…
And now web3 / and metaverse
Benedict Evans —— January 2022 30

**Layout**

Centred three-circle composition on a light grey field: the title is centred at the top, with a one-line subtitle beneath it, and three equal circles sit in a horizontal row across the middle of the slide. The circles are set at the same vertical level, with the third circle offset slightly lower-right in its text. A small footer line sits bottom-right with the author name, date and slide number, and no slide number is set at the far corner.

**Visuals**

Three filled circles arranged left to right as a row of equal-size flat shapes; no photographs, charts or logos. Circle 1 (left) is dark charcoal #535353 with white text 'Internet, web, / open source, / smartphones'. Circle 2 (centre) is dark charcoal #535353 with white text 'But also drones, / 3D printing, / games consoles…'. Circle 3 (right) is coral red #EB483F with white text 'And now web3 / and metaverse'. All circles are solid fills with no outline, no shadow and no gradient, each about 22.1% of slide width and 39.3% of slide height, perfectly round.

**Design**

Background light grey #F1F1F1 with no texture. Title and subtitle in a serif face resembling Photina (regular weight), title in grey #545454 and subtitle in grey #545454. Body text inside circles is white #FFFFFF in the same serif face, regular weight. Accent is coral red #EB483F on the third circle only. Title is about 5.6% of slide height, subtitle about 2.6%, circle text about 3.3%. ### Slide 31

**Text**

The future can take a very long time / Mobile data didn’t take off outside Japan until a decade after the dream began / Smartphone unit sales (m) / Proto-smartphones* / iPhone & Android / 1,500 / 1,250 / 1,000 / 750 / 500 / 250 / 0 / 2000 / 2005 / 2010 / 2015 / Benedict Evans —— January 2022 / 31 / Source: IDC, Companies / * S60, Palm, RIM, Windows Phone

**Layout**

Full-width light grey (#F1F1F1) header band holds a left-aligned title at about 20% from the left edge, with a subtitle beneath it. A white content band runs from about 20.5% to 86% of the slide height and holds a single line chart whose plot starts at about 20% of the width, with a legend at the far left (about 4.8% from the left edge) and a chart title above the plot. A grey footer band below holds the source note aligned with the chart’s left edge (about 20.5%), and the author, date and page number on the right, with the page number at about 94.4% from the left edge.

**Visuals**

One line chart. Title: Smartphone unit sales (m), bold sans-serif above the plot. Y-axis: unit sales in millions, range 0 to 1,500, labelled ticks at 0, 250, 500, 750, 1,000, 1,250 and 1,500, with light grey dotted horizontal gridlines at each labelled tick. X-axis: years 2000 to 2015, labelled ticks at 2000, 2005, 2010 and 2015, with a thin grey baseline at 0. Legend at upper left of the plot: dark grey line marked Proto-smartphones* and red line marked iPhone & Android. Series 1, Proto-smartphones* (dark grey #545454, solid, about 3 px stroke, smoothed): starts at about 0 in 2001, about 10 in 2003, about 50 in 2005, about 70 in 2007, about 100 in 2009, peaks at about 150 around 2010, about 130 in 2011, about 50 in 2012 and ends at about 25 in 2013. Series 2, iPhone & Android (red #E8453C, solid, about 3 px stroke, smoothed): starts at about 5 in 2007, about 25 in 2009, about 100 in 2010, about 250 in 2011, about 500 in 2012, about 950 in 2013, about 1,250 in 2014 and ends at about 1,420 in 2015. No values are printed on the chart, so all series values are estimates. No highlighted points. The two series cross at about 2010 to 2011.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 covering the full slide, with a white #FFFFFF band from about 20.5% to 86.1% of the height. Flat, minimal look with no gradients, shadows or corner radii. Background light grey #F1F1F1 for the header and footer bands and white #FFFFFF for the chart band. Title in a regular high-contrast serif (Photina-style, close to Century/Georgia serif) at about 5.6% of slide height, colour #545454. Subtitle in the same serif at about 2.6% of slide height, colour #545454. Chart title and body labels in a neutral grotesque sans-serif (Helvetica Neue). Chart title bold at about 2.2%; axis, legend and label text regular at about 1.85%, colour #545454 to #535353. Footer author name in bold sans-serif, date and page number regular, all in light grey #A6A6A6 at about 1.85%. Source note in regular sans-serif at about 1.8%, colour #545454. Accent colours are the dark grey #545454 for the first series and red #E8453C for the second series. Gridlines are thin dotted light grey lines. Line strokes are about 3 px with round joins.

### Slide 32

**Text**

And you can have a winter (or two) on the way
Both AI and VR went through prolonged ‘Winters’ as the tech lagged behind early enthusiasm
Term frequency in English-language books
Artificial Intelligence
Virtual Reality
1950
1960
1970
1980
1990
2000
2010
2019
Source: Google Ngram
Benedict Evans — January 2022
32

**Layout**

Full-width light grey (#F1F1F1) header band at top holding a left-aligned serif title and subtitle starting at about x 20% with a 20% left margin, a white central band containing a line chart with its title above the plot and a legend at the far left, and a light grey footer band holding the source line at left and the author, date and page number at right. The page number sits at the bottom right at about x 94%.

**Visuals**

Line chart titled 'Term frequency in English-language books', with no units printed on the y-axis and no y-axis labels. X-axis runs 1950 to 2019 with labels every 10 years (1950, 1960, 1970, 1980, 1990, 2000, 2010, 2019). Y-axis is unlabelled; faint dotted horizontal gridlines at about four levels above the baseline. Series 1, Artificial Intelligence, dark grey line (#545454) about 2 units near 1950-1958, rising gradually to about 15% of plot height by 1980, peaking at about 90% of plot height around 1987, falling to about 35% by 2000, about 25% near 2010, rising to about 95% at 2019. Series 2, Virtual Reality, red line (about #E84C3D) near zero until about 1988, rising sharply to a peak of about 35% of plot height around 1996, dipping to about 20% near 2007, then rising to about 60% at 2019. No values are printed on the slide, so all values are estimates. Legend at far left with a short dark grey dash labelled Artificial Intelligence and a short red dash labelled Virtual Reality. No highlighted points. Source line reads Source: Google Ngram.

**Design**

Background: Light grey #F1F1F1 full-bleed, with a white #FFFFFF band from 20.5% to 86% of slide height. Light grey #F1F1F1 header and footer bands with a white middle band. Titles in dark grey #545454, body and axis text in dark grey #545454 and #535353, footer author and page number in light grey #A6A6A6. Title in a PhotinaMT-style serif roman at about 5.6% of slide height; subtitle in the same serif at about 2.6%. Chart title and axis labels in HelveticaNeue sans-serif, chart title bold at about 2.2%, axis labels regular at about 1.85%. Lines are about 2 px thick, rounded joins, no shadows, no gradients, gridlines thin dotted grey. Baseline is a thin grey solid line.

### Slide 33

**Text**

No-one knows anything / In 1995, the future shape of the ‘internet’ still looked very unclear / Is it just the internet, or will there / still be lots of other networks? / How will FTP, Gopher, Telnet, / WAIS and Web interact? / How will the web work? Should / the browser be an editor? A / server? Should it have images? / Source: Telegeography / Benedict Evans –– January 2022 / 33

**Layout**

Title and subtitle are left-aligned at the top, starting at about x 20 percent, with a figure boxed on the left and a three-paragraph text block to its right at about x 69 percent, vertically centred on the figure. Source line sits bottom left and the footer with author, date and slide number sits bottom right on one baseline near y 91 percent.

**Visuals**

A bordered black-and-white reproduction of a figure titled 'Figure 5. Network Gateways: A Cyberspace Divided' with a caption paragraph, a TeleGeography, Inc. 1995 copyright mark, and a network diagram of irregular blob-shaped outlines with labels: 'Other Nets' containing 'private' and 'secure'; 'The Matrix' (large blob) containing 'Usenet' (diamond), 'UUCP' (blob), 'Minitel' (blob), 'Private Nets' (blob), 'FidoNet' (blob), 'Internet' containing 'Telnet space', 'FTP space', 'Gopher space', 'Web space', 'WAIS space'; 'BITNET' (blob); 'Commercial Services' containing 'America Online', 'Delphi', 'Prodigy', 'GEnie', 'Compuserve'. Dashed double-headed arrows with small triangular gateway symbols connect the blobs; a legend at bottom left shows a double-headed arrow with a triangle and the label 'Network Gateway'. Figure has a thin black border with black corner marks at top left and bottom right, sits on a white card with a faint drop shadow. Monochrome, black on white, no tint.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1. Light grey background #F1F1F1. Title and subtitle in a serif (Photina-like) in grey #545454. Body text serif #545454. Source and footer in Helvetica Neue sans-serif, grey #545454 for source and #A6A6A6 for footer. Title about 5.6 percent of slide height, subtitle 2.6 percent, body 3.0 percent, footer 1.9 percent. No lines or shape fills other than the figure's white card and black corner marks.

Left-aligned title and subtitle start at x 20.1% with a generous empty left margin of about 20%. ### Slide 37

**Text**

‘Digital transformation’
Sounds like a parody of marketing gibberish - but describes a generational change in enterprise tech
Mainframes
Client server,
PCs, Oracle,
Microsoft
Cloud, SaaS,
machine learning
Benedict Evans
–– January 2022
37

**Layout**

Left-aligned title and subtitle block starting at about x 20% below the top margin, with the subtitle directly under the title. Three equal circles sit in a single horizontal row across the centre-lower area of the slide, joined by two arrows, with the footer line bottom right: author name, date and slide number on one baseline at about y 91%.

**Visuals**

Diagram: three filled circles of equal size (each about 22.1% wide by 39.3% high) in a left-to-right row, connected by two right-pointing block arrows. Circle 1 is dark grey #535353 and contains the label 'Mainframes'. Circle 2 is dark grey #535353 and contains the label 'Client server, PCs, Oracle, Microsoft' on four lines. Circle 3 is red #EB483F and contains 'Cloud, SaaS, machine learning' on two lines. Two block arrows, fill #A7A7A7, flat, no outline, sit in the gaps between circles at about x 41.3% and 68.9%, each about 5.2% wide and 9.3% high, pointing right. No photographs, logos or icons.

**Design**

Background light grey #F1F1F1, flat with no gradient or pattern. **Layout**

Single content slide with a full-width light grey header band holding the title and subtitle, a white body band in the middle holding a legend at far left and one stacked column chart spanning the right of the body, and a light grey footer band with the source and footnote at left and the author, date and page number at right. Title and subtitle are left-aligned at x 20.1% on the same left edge as the chart label; the left margin of the legend is 4.8%, the right margin about 5.6%.

**Visuals**

One stacked column chart titled 'Annual capex ($bn)' (bold sans, dark grey). Y axis: 0 to 150 in steps of 25, labelled values 0, 25, 50, 75, 100, 125, 150, no unit suffix on tick labels (unit given in chart title as $bn), faint dotted horizontal gridlines at each step. X axis: categories 2010, 2011, 2012, 2013, 2014, 2015, 2016, 2017, 2018, 2019, 2020, Sept 21 TTM, with a thin solid grey baseline at 0. Four stacked series, bottom to top: Alphabet (red), Microsoft (pale pink), Amazon* (dark grey), Meta (light grey). No values are printed on the bars; the following totals and segment heights are estimates marked about: 2010 about 4 Alphabet, about 1 Microsoft, about 2 Amazon, about 0 Meta (total about 7); 2011 about 3, about 3, about 3, about 0 (total about 9); 2012 about 3, about 2, about 5, about 1 (total about 11); 2013 about 7, about 4, about 6, about 2 (total about 19); 2014 about 11, about 5, about 9, about 2 (total about 27); 2015 about 10, about 6, about 9, about 3 (total about 28); 2016 about 10, about 8, about 14, about 4 (total about 36); 2017 about 13, about 8, about 23, about 7 (total about 51); 2018 about 25, about 11, about 26, about 14 (total about 76); 2019 about 24, about 14, about 27, about 15 (total about 80); 2020 about 22, about 16, about 49, about 15 (total about 102); Sept 21 TTM about 24, about 19, about 65, about 17 (total about 125). Legend at upper left with four swatches in top-to-bottom order Meta, Amazon*, Microsoft, Alphabet. No highlighted points. Source and footnote are beneath the chart; no photograph, logo or icon.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide, with a white #FFFFFF band from 20.5% to 86.1% of slide height. Flat, minimal, two-tone grey frame with white body. Header and footer bands #F1F1F1 with no border or rule; body band white #FFFFFF. Title in a light regular Photina-style serif, dark grey #545454, about 5.6% of slide height (cap height about 3.9%). Subtitle in the same serif regular, #545454, about 2.6% of slide height. Chart label and axis text in Helvetica Neue regular, #545454 to #535353, about 1.9% of slide height; chart label in Helvetica Neue bold #545554 at about 2.2%. Footer text Helvetica Neue regular #545454 at about 1.9%; author name Helvetica Neue bold #A6A6A6 and date regular #A6A6A6; page number regular #A6A6A6. Chart colours: Alphabet red #EA4335, Microsoft pale pink #F9D9D6, Amazon dark grey #535353, Meta light grey #A6A6A6. Thin white separators between stacked segments. Gridlines dotted light grey #A6A6A6 about 0.1% stroke; baseline solid grey. No shadows, gradients, rounded corners or borders.

**Layout**

Two-band header and footer on a light grey field: the title sits top-left at about 20% from the left edge, with the subtitle beneath it, and a white content panel spans the full width from about 20.5% to 86% of the height. The chart is left-aligned to a plot area from about 20% to 95% of the width, with a legend at the far left margin and source line at lower left; the author line, date and slide number 42 sit right-aligned in the footer band.

**Visuals**

Dot-plot chart (scatter of categorical dots), titled 'Average number of SaaS applications per Okta customer, by industry'. Y-axis: 'Average number of SaaS applications' is not printed; tick labels run 0 to 175 in steps of 25, with faint dotted horizontal gridlines at each tick. X-axis: categories Tech, Media, Retail, Professional Services, Finance, Manufacturing, Healthcare, Construction, Govt, Energy, with a solid thin grey baseline at 0. Four series, each one dot per category: 2020 red #EE4035, 2019 dark grey #4D4D4D, 2018 mid grey #A6A6A6, 2017 light grey #D9D9D9. No values are printed on the dots; approximate values read from positions: Tech 2020 about 155, 2019 about 133, 2018 about 122, 2017 about 99; Media 2020 about 133, 2019 about 124, 2018 about 114, 2017 about 110; Retail 2020 about 120, 2019 about 112, 2018 about 96, 2017 about 76; Professional Services 2020 about 116, 2019 about 85, 2018 about 67, 2017 about 64; Finance 2020 about 104, 2019 about 88, 2018 about 73, 2017 about 70; Manufacturing 2020 about 98, 2019 about 96, 2018 about 93, 2017 about 92; Healthcare 2020 about 96, 2019 about 81, 2018 about 66, 2017 about 56; Construction 2020 about 83, 2019 about 72, 2018 about 66, 2017 about 64; Govt 2020 about 76, 2019 about 55, 2018 about 45, 2017 about 33; Energy 2020 about 54, 2019 about 51, 2018 about 48, 2017 about 41. Legend at left: red dot 2020, dark grey dot 2019, mid grey dot 2018, light grey dot 2017, listed top to bottom. Highlighted points: none; the 2020 series is the only red series and is drawn on top of the others. Dots overlap where values are close (e.g. Manufacturing, Energy).

**Design**

Background: Light grey #F1F1F1 full-slide background, with a white #FFFFFF panel from about 20.5% to 86.1% of the height. Flat, minimal look on light grey #F1F1F1 with a white content panel. Title in a serif (Photina-style, regular weight) in dark grey #545454, about 5.6% of slide height for the title; subtitle in the same serif at about 2.6%. Chart title and axis labels in a Helvetica Neue sans-serif; chart title bold #545554 at about 2.2%; tick and category labels regular #545454 at about 1.85%. Accent colour is red #EE4035 for the 2020 series; other series are greyscale. Gridlines are thin dotted grey; baseline is a thin solid grey line. Dots are solid circles with no outline or shadow. No gradients, no corner radii.

43

**Layout**

Full-width slide with a light grey header band holding the title and subtitle left-aligned at about 20% of the slide width, a white content band below it holding the chart title and a dot plot with a y-axis at left and category labels beneath, and a light grey footer band holding the source note at left and the author, date and slide number at right. The legend sits in the left margin at about 4% of slide width. The slide number 43 sits at the right edge of the footer at about 94% of slide width.

**Visuals**

One chart: dot plot (scatter of single values per category, no connecting lines). Chart title: 'Average number of SaaS applications per team'. Y-axis: numeric scale 0 to 80, labelled at 10-unit intervals, unit is number of SaaS applications per team (axis title not printed), faint dotted horizontal gridlines at each 10 units. X-axis: categories Security, Engineering, Product, Infotech, Customer success, Marketing, Finance, Sales, Ops, HR; solid thin grey baseline at 0. Three series with circular markers about 1.8% of slide width in diameter: 2021 red markers, 2020 dark grey markers, 2019 light grey markers. No values are printed on the chart, so values are estimates marked about: Security 2021 about 73, 2020 about 63, 2019 about 55; Engineering 2021 about 70, 2020 about 62, 2019 about 47; Product 2021 about 65, 2020 about 63, 2019 about 40; Infotech 2021 about 61, 2020 about 59, 2019 about 49; Customer success 2021 about 57, 2020 about 52, 2019 about 39; Marketing 2021 about 52, 2020 about 48, 2019 about 37; Finance 2021 about 51, 2020 about 46, 2019 about 39; Sales 2021 about 50, 2020 about 49, 2019 about 45; Ops 2021 about 50, 2020 about 49, 2019 about 46; HR 2021 about 49, 2020 about 47, 2019 about 38. Legend at left: red dot 2021, dark grey dot 2020, light grey dot 2019, stacked vertically. No highlighted points. Markers overlap where values are close, with red drawn on top of dark grey and dark grey on top of light grey.

**Design**

Background: Light grey #F1F1F1 full-bleed, with a white #FFFFFF band across the middle of the slide. Flat, minimal, no gradients, shadows or rounded corners. Header and footer bands are light grey #F1F1F1 and the content band is white #FFFFFF. Title in a light serif (PhotinaMT-style) in dark grey #545454, about 5.6% of slide height. Subtitle in the same serif, dark grey #545454, about 2.6% of slide height. Chart title in bold sans-serif (Helvetica Neue Bold), dark grey #545554, about 2.2% of slide height. Chart labels and legend in regular sans-serif (Helvetica Neue), #545554 and #535353, about 1.9% of slide height. Footer source text in regular sans-serif #545454. Footer author name in bold sans-serif #A6A6A6, date and slide number in regular sans-serif #A6A6A6. Accent colours: red #EA4335 for 2021, dark grey #4D4D4D for 2020, light grey #A6A6A6 for 2019. Gridlines are dotted light grey #BFBFBF, hairline. Axis baseline is a solid thin grey line #8C8C8C.

### Slide 44

**Text**

The cloud means workflows, not better file shares
Don’t put your spreadsheet on the web - find a better way to do the job
Benedict Evans
–– January 2022
44

**Layout**

Single open layout with no grid or columns: title and subtitle are left-aligned at x 20.1% on a common left margin, the three logo images sit in a horizontal row across the middle-lower area connected by two grey arrows, and the footer line with author, date and slide number sits bottom right at y 91.1%. The title sits at top (y 5.5%), subtitle directly beneath it (y 13.1%), and the slide number is at x 94.4% at the right margin.

**Visuals**

Three brand logos in a left-to-right flow connected by two grey block arrows. Left: Microsoft Excel icon, green (#1D7145 approx.) rounded spreadsheet grid with a dark green X panel, about 17.5% wide, placed at x 41.3%? no: placed at x 20.1%, y 36.8%, 17.5% by 31.1% (337 by 337 px image). Centre: Google Sheets document icon, bright green (#23A566 approx.) page with folded top-right corner (pale green #8FD6AD) and white table grid, placed at x 41.3%, y 54.0%, 32.4% by 32.0% region (577 by 320 px image) as a full-colour logo. Right: Figma logo, five-part rounded stack in red-orange #F24E1E, coral #FF7262, purple #A259FF, blue #1ABCFE and green #0ACF83, placed at x 77.9%, y 39.7%, 10.6% by 28.2%. Between Excel and Sheets is a grey (#A7A7A7) block arrow pointing down-right at x 42.1%, y 57.3%, 5.0% by 8.2%. Between Sheets and Figma is a second grey (#A7A7A7) block arrow pointing up-right at about x 61.5%, y 57.3%. No charts, photos or text inside visuals.

**Design**

Background flat light grey #F1F1F1 with no gradient or texture. Title in a light serif (Photina-style, Photina MT regular) in dark grey #545454, large, about 5.6% of slide height. Subtitle in the same serif, regular weight, #545454, about 2.6% of slide height. Footer in Helvetica Neue: author name bold #A6A6A6 about 1.85% of slide height, date and slide number regular #A6A6A6. Arrows are flat grey #A7A7A7 block shapes with no shadow. Logos are flat with no shadows or outlines.

Below the frame are a timeline with small coloured avatar markers, a play button with timecode 00:10 / 00:30, a filmstrip of purple scene thumbnails, the filename Zero_Gravity_Room.mp4 with 'John · 1 day ago', four avatar icons, tabs 'Comments' (active, underlined) and 'File info', and two comments: Megan 45s ago 'Can we brighten up the color on the rail here?' and Scott Timber 1h 'This shot is really cool, can we push it to the beginning?' each with Reply and a thumbs-up. 46

**Layout**

Two equal columns split at the vertical centre: a grey left half with the title and subtitle at a left margin of about 4.6% of slide width, and a white right half holding a device mockup centred in the right column. The source line sits bottom-left at about 91% height, and the footer (author, date, page number) runs along the bottom of the right column, with the page number at the far right near x 94%. No header and no slide number other than the footer one.

**Visuals**

Photograph-style composite illustration of a tablet and a phone showing a video-editing app. Tablet (large, portrait, dark bezel with front camera at top centre, status bar reading 12:51 Tue Jan 27th and 100% battery): the screen shows a video frame of a surreal rocky canyon with a narrow stone stair and a glowing pink/magenta walkway, lit in cyan, violet and magenta, with a stylised thick teal arrow pointing down-right onto the scene and a thin red-orange circle around a small glowing structure. Below the frame is a timeline strip of small coloured avatar markers joined by thin pink and green lines, a play button in a dark circle, timecode '00:10 / 00:30', a row of filmstrip thumbnails of the same canyon, the file name Zero_Gravity_Room.mp4, the line 'John · 1 day ago', a row of four small circular avatars, the tabs Comments (underlined) and File info, and two comments: Megan, 45s ago, 'Can we brighten up the color on the rail here?' with a blue dot, Reply and a thumbs-up; Scott Timber, 1h, 'This shot is really cool, can we push it to the beginning?' with Reply and a thumbs-up. Phone (smaller, overlapping the tablet's lower-right corner, black bezel with notch, status bar 12:51): dark app screen showing the Yambo logo and wordmark, a search icon and avatar at top, 'Recently updated' with three thumbnail cards (Zero_Gravity.mp4, Apple Music, a partly visible third card), 'Updated assets' with 'Today' and two list rows: blast_off.mov (05:10, Design feedback, 1h, 2 comments) and Rays of Light.mov (01:20, Design feedback). Composition: the devices are shown straight-on, unrotated, full object visible, not cropped, boxed on white with no shadow visible. Lighting: even, screen-lit. Dominant colours: near-black device bodies, violet #3B2A6B, magenta #E0289A, cyan #2EC4D6, teal arrow #3FD6D0, red-orange circle #E0463A, dark UI panels #1C1D27.

**Design**

Background: Split background: left half solid light grey #F2F2F2, right half solid white #FFFFFF. Minimal, two-tone split layout. Left background light grey; right half pure white. Titles and body set in a book serif (Photina-style, regular weight, not bold), title in dark grey #545454, subtitle also #545454. Footer and source line in a neutral Helvetica-style sans-serif, regular weight, small; author name bold in #A6A6A6 and the rest of the footer in #A6A6A6. Title size about 5.6% of slide height, subtitle about 3.0%, footer and source about 1.9%. Shapes: two flat rectangles, no borders, no gradients, no shadows. ### Slide 47

**Text**

Remaking companies / Vodafone had 2.6m invoices, 40k queries & 1k payments FTEs - how would you change that? / Applying digital: Purchase to Pay process / 2.6m invoices a year / €38bn payments / 40K queries / Process / Traditional approach / Digital / Mix of e-invoices + paper invoices / Manual paper invoice processing / Mix of e-invoices + paper invoices / Intelligent OCR and robotics / Automatic invoice processing / 2.6m invoices a year / Before: > 1,000 FTEs managed supplier payments worldwide / Now: 400 FTEs in Shared Services / Target: <100 next year / C2 General / 6 / Source: Vodafone (2019) / Benedict Evans / January 2022 / 47

**Layout**

Slide title 'Remaking companies' sits top-left at about x 20% y 5.5%, with the subtitle question beneath it at y 13%, both left-aligned on a shared left margin at x 20%. A framed white-topped screenshot slide (embedded image, about x 20% y 21% w 66% h 66%) sits centred below, with a 'Source: Vodafone (2019)' line bottom-left and 'Benedict Evans — January 2022' with slide number 47 bottom-right at y 91%.

**Visuals**

An embedded screenshot of a Vodafone slide, full rectangle with a soft drop shadow, not cropped. Top band white with the heading 'Applying digital: Purchase to Pay process' in red, 'Applying digital:' bold and 'Purchase to Pay process' regular. Second band light grey with a faint stippled wave pattern containing three icon-and-label stats: an envelope with a red badge icon with '2.6m invoices a year', a stack of coins with a dollar symbol icon with '€38bn payments', and two speech bubbles with a red queries icon with '40K queries'. Below, a three-column process diagram: Process column with a black document-with-pencil icon and red '2.6m' above 'invoices a year' text; Traditional approach column with a red circle containing a white envelope, a red chevron arrow pointing right, and a group of people icon, followed by two white boxes 'Mix of e-invoices + paper invoices' and 'Manual paper invoice processing' with a red arrow beneath pointing right; Digital column with a red circle containing a white envelope, a red chevron, and a robot icon, followed by three white boxes 'Mix of e-invoices + paper invoices', 'Intelligent OCR and robotics' and 'Automatic invoice processing' with a red arrow beneath pointing right. Each column has a bold black heading with a thin red underline. A right-hand white panel with a red top and bottom rule holds three red-bulleted statements. Colours: red #E60000, white, black #1A1A1A, light grey #F1F1F1 with stipple #D9D9D9. Vodafone red logo bottom-right of the screenshot and a small 'C2 General' label bottom-left, with page number 6 beside the logo.

**Design**

Background light grey #F1F1F1. Titles in PhotinaMT-style serif, regular weight, colour #545454, title about 5.6% of slide height, subtitle about 2.6%. Footer in HelveticaNeue regular, grey #A6A6A6 for the name and date, with Benedict Evans in bold, about 1.9% of slide height; source line in HelveticaNeue #545454. Screenshot uses Helvetica-style sans-serif bold and regular in black and red #E60000. Thin red rules, rounded red circles, no gradients on the slide itself.

### Slide 48

**Review notes**

- What would change the score: Replace the repeated three-circle diagrams (slides 13, 18, 21, 30, 48) with more varied or more informative visuals, and trim the near-empty divider slides.

**Text**

Mass deployment / Thousands more companies deploying everything we learned in the last decade, in ever more places / Platforms / SaaS, cloud, mobile / Building blocks / Networks, / marketplaces, / workflows, / machine learning / Creation / So many more / companies to / build / Benedict Evans –– January 2022 / 48

**Layout**

Three white circles sit in a single horizontal row across the middle of the slide, evenly spaced, with the title and subtitle left-aligned at the top starting at about 20% of the width. The footer line and page number are right-aligned along the bottom margin at about 91% of the slide height.

**Visuals**

Three identical-sized outlined circles in a row, each about 21.7% wide and 38.5% tall, with white fill. The first two circles have a dark grey stroke (#545454, about 2 px). The third circle, on the right, has a red stroke (about #E8453C, about 2 px) to highlight it. Circle 1 contains the heading Platforms and the line SaaS, cloud, mobile. Circle 2 contains the heading Building blocks and the lines Networks, marketplaces, workflows, machine learning. Circle 3 contains the heading Creation and the lines So many more, companies to, build. No photographs, charts, or logos.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1. Flat light grey background with no gradient or texture. Title and body text in a dark grey (#545454). Headings in the circles are bold serif. The footer name is bold sans-serif in light grey (#A6A6A6). Serif typeface is Photina (roman and SemiBold), a transitional serif. Sans-serif footer is Helvetica Neue (Bold for name, Regular for date and number). Title is about 5.6% of slide height; subtitle about 2.6%; circle headings about 3.3%; circle body about 3.0%; footer about 1.9%. Circles have no shadows; the strokes are the only shape styling. Accent colour is red #E8453C used only on the third circle outline.

When do the atoms stop mattering?

100
75
50
25
0
2000
2005
2010

Source: BLS, US Census, Companies

51

**Layout**

Full-width slide with a light grey header band holding the title and subtitle, a white middle band holding a single line chart with its title above the plot and a legend at the far left, and a light grey footer band holding the source line at left and the author, date and page number at right. Title and subtitle are left-aligned at x 20.1%; the chart and source share a left edge near x 20.5%; the footer author line and page number end near x 95.6%.

**Visuals**

Line chart titled 'Decline from peak, USA (indexed)'. Type: line chart with three smoothed series. Y axis: 0 to 100, indexed value, gridlines and labels at 0, 25, 50, 75 and 100, no unit beyond index. X axis: years 2000 to 2020, labels at 2000, 2005, 2010, 2015 and 2020. Series: Pay TV subs, light grey line about #A6A6A6, starts at about 100 in 2009 and stays near 100 to about 2011, about 96 in 2014, about 87 in 2017, about 76 in 2019, ends about 68 in 2020. Book store revenue, mid grey line about #545454, starts about 100 in 2007, about 92 in 2009, about 81 in 2011, about 67 in 2013, about 62 in 2015, about 55 in 2019, ends about 38 in 2020. Newspaper ad revenue, black line about #000000, starts at 100 in 2000, about 91 in 2001, about 89 in 2003, about 91 in 2004, about 90 in 2006, about 71 in 2008, about 54 in 2009, about 42 in 2012, about 32 in 2014, about 25 in 2017, ends about 18 in 2020. Legend at far left in order Pay TV subs, Book store revenue, Newspaper ad revenue, each with a short line swatch in its series colour. No highlighted points. Gridlines are thin dotted light grey lines at each labelled value; the zero baseline is a solid thin grey line.

**Design**

Background light grey #F1F1F1 for header and footer bands, white #FFFFFF for the chart band. Title in Photina serif regular, dark grey #545454, about 5.6% of slide height. Subtitle in Photina serif regular, #545454, about 2.6% of slide height. Chart title and all chart labels, legend and footer text in Helvetica Neue sans-serif, about 1.9% to 2.2% of slide height; chart title bold, labels regular. Author name bold in light grey #A6A6A6; date and page number regular in #A6A6A6. Source line regular #545454. Lines are flat with no shadows, gradients or rounded corners; series lines about 2 px wide, gridlines about 1 px dotted.

### Slide 52

**Text**

And how fast? / If pay TV fell ‘gradually, then suddenly’, retail has been a boiled frog / Decline from peak, USA (indexed) / Pay TV subs / 100 / Book store revenue / Newspaper ad revenue / Offline share of retail / 75 / 50 / 25 / 0 / 2000 / 2005 / 2010 / 2015 / 2020 / Source: BLS, US Census, Companies / Benedict Evans / —— January 2022 / 52

**Layout**

Full-slide layout with a light grey (#F1F1F1) header band across the top about 20% of the height and a matching grey footer band from about 86% to the bottom, framing a white central panel. The title and subtitle are left-aligned at x 20.1% in the header, with the title above the subtitle. The chart occupies the white panel from x about 4.8% to 96%, with the chart title left-aligned at x 20.5% and the legend at the far left (x 4.8%). The source line sits at x 20.5% in the footer, the author, date and slide number are right-side footer items ending at about 96%, and the slide number is at the far right (x 94.4%).

**Visuals**

Line chart titled 'Decline from peak, USA (indexed)'. Y axis: index from 0 to 100, labelled gridlines at 0, 25, 50, 75 and 100, with no unit label shown. X axis: years 2000 to 2020, labelled every five years (2000, 2005, 2010, 2015, 2020). Four series, smooth lines: Pay TV subs (light grey #A6A6A6-ish line, starts at 100 in 2009, about 96 in 2014, about 88 in 2016, about 76 in 2019, ends about 68 in 2020); Book store revenue (dark grey #545454 line, starts at 100 in 2007, about 92 in 2009, about 72 in 2012, about 66 in 2014, about 60 in 2018, about 53 in 2019, ends about 38 in 2020); Newspaper ad revenue (black line, starts at 100 in 2000, about 91 in 2001, about 89 in 2003, about 91 in 2004, about 85 in 2007, about 54 in 2009, about 42 in 2011, about 30 in 2015, about 18 in 2020); Offline share of retail (red #E8473B line, starts about 98 in 2000, about 96 in 2007, about 92 in 2015, about 86 in 2019, ends about 82 in 2020). No data labels printed. Legend at left with four entries, each a short coloured line swatch followed by its label, in the order Pay TV subs, Book store revenue, Newspaper ad revenue, Offline share of retail. No points are highlighted. Gridlines are thin dotted light grey horizontal lines at each labelled value; the baseline at 0 is a solid thin grey line.

**Design**

Background is #F1F1F1 for the header and footer bands with a #FFFFFF central panel. Title 'And how fast?' is a serif (Photina-style Didone-like transitional serif), regular weight, colour #545454, about 5.6% of slide height. Subtitle is the same serif, regular, #545454, about 2.6% of slide height. Chart title is bold sans-serif (Helvetica Neue Bold), #545554, about 2.2% of slide height. Legend and axis labels are Helvetica Neue regular, about 1.85% of slide height, colours #535353 and #545554. Source line is Helvetica Neue regular #545454, 1.85%. Footer author name is Helvetica Neue Bold #A6A6A6, date and slide number Helvetica Neue regular #A6A6A6, 1.85%. Series colours: Pay TV subs #A6A6A6, Book store revenue #545454, Newspaper ad revenue #000000, Offline share of retail #F04E3E-style red.
