### Slide 53

**Text**

The great unbundling / “The internet breaks apart old value chains and creates new ones” - and we could have said that in 1995 / Value chains & / gate-keeping / based on physical / break apart / New models / New entrants / New channels / Destabilisation / New gatekeepers / New winners / Benedict Evans –– January 2022 / 53

**Layout**

Single-column title block top-left at x 20.1% aligned left, with a bold-to-regular subtitle line beneath it. Three large circles sit in a horizontal row across the middle of the slide, connected by two grey block arrows, with the footer line at bottom right and the slide number at the far right of the footer.

**Visuals**

Diagram: a left-to-right process of three filled circles joined by two grey right-pointing block arrows. Circle 1 (dark grey #535353, x 19.2%, y 33.9%, w 22.1%, h 39.3%) reads 'Value chains & / gate-keeping / based on physical / break apart'. Arrow 1 (grey #A7A7A7, x 41.4%, y 48.9%, w 5.2%, h 9.3%) points from circle 1 to circle 2. Circle 2 (dark grey #535353, x 46.7%, y 33.9%, w 22.1%, h 39.3%) reads 'New models / New entrants / New channels / Destabilisation'. Arrow 2 (grey #A7A7A7, x 68.9%, y 48.9%, w 5.2%, h 9.3%) points from circle 2 to circle 3. Circle 3 (red #EB483F, x 74.1%, y 33.9%, w 22.1%, h 39.3%) is the highlighted endpoint and reads 'New gatekeepers / New winners'. No photographs, logos or icons.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1. Light grey background #F1F1F1 with flat styling and no gradients or shadows. Title and subtitle in a serif face (PhotinaMT-like, regular weight) in grey #545454; title is about 5.6% of slide height, subtitle about 2.6%. Circle body text is white #FFFFFF serif (PhotinaMT-like, regular) about 3.3% of slide height, centred. Accent is red #EB483F on the final circle. Footer in Helvetica Neue, about 1.85% of slide height, colour #A6A6A6; 'Benedict Evans' bold, the rest regular. Arrows are flat grey #A7A7A7 block arrows with no outline.

### Slide 54

**Text**

TV unbundles…
US pay TV penetration is down a third from the peak
US TV household penetration
TV
Pay TV
Netflix streaming

50%
25%
0%
1940
1960

Source: Company data, FCC, US Census, Netflix, @BenedictEvans
Benedict Evans  ––  January 2022
54

**Layout**

Full-slide light grey band framing a white content band that runs from about 20% to 86% of the slide height. The title and subtitle are left-aligned at about 20% of the slide width, with the chart title, chart and legend sitting below them. The source line sits at the left of the footer band and the author, date and slide number sit at the right, with the slide number at the far right margin.

**Visuals**

Line chart titled US TV household penetration. Y axis: household penetration, 0% to 100%, gridlines and labels at 0%, 25%, 50%, 75% and 100%. X axis: year, 1940 to 2020, labels at 1940, 1960, 1980, 2000 and 2020. Series TV (light grey line, about #A6A6A6): begins about 1946 near 0%, rises steeply to about 80% by 1953 and about 90% by 1963, reaches about 98% by 1985, then runs flat at about 98 to 99% through 2020. Series Pay TV (red line, about #E8483C): starts about 1956 near 0%, about 2% by 1965, about 18% by 1980, about 42% by 1985, about 67% by 1995, about 80% by 2004, peak about 85% around 2009 to 2010, falls to about 57% by 2020. Series Netflix streaming (dark grey line, about #4D4D4D): starts about 2011 near 17%, about 32% by 2014, about 47% by 2018, ends about 52% in 2020. Legend at upper left of the chart with short line swatches: TV (grey), Pay TV (red), Netflix streaming (dark grey). No highlighted points, no annotations.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide, with a white #FFFFFF band from about 20.5% to 86.1% of slide height. Light grey #F1F1F1 bands at top and bottom of the slide with a white middle band. Title in a light serif (PhotinaMT regular) at about 5.6% of slide height, colour #545454. Subtitle in the same serif at about 2.6% of slide height, colour #545454. Chart title and series-name text in bold and regular Helvetica Neue sans-serif at about 2.2% and 1.85% of slide height. Axis labels and legend in Helvetica Neue regular, colour #545454 to #535353. Footer text in Helvetica Neue, source line #545454, author and date in grey #A6A6A6, author name bold. Series lines are about 2 px wide, with light dotted grey horizontal gridlines. No shadows, gradients or rounded corners.

**Text**

New entrants and aggregators emerge / New entrants outspending most legacy content manufacturers / 2021e content budgets ($bn) / Sport / Film & TV / 35 / 30 / 25 / 20 / 15 / 10 / 5 / 0 / Disney / Netflix / Comcast / Amazon / ViacomCBS / Warner / Discovery / Fox / Apple / UK, FR, DE, ES, IT combined / Source: Morgan Stanley, Ampere / Benedict Evans –– January 2022 56

**Layout**

Full-bleed light-grey slide split into a top grey title band (about 0 to 20.5% of height), a white content area (about 20.5% to 86% of height) and a bottom grey footer band (about 86% to 100%). The title and subtitle are left-aligned at x 20.1%, the chart title and chart sit in the white band with the y-axis at about x 18.6% and the bars spanning to about x 93%, and the legend sits at the far left (x about 4.9%). The source line sits left at x 20.5% in the footer, the author, date and slide number sit right-aligned at the bottom right (slide number at x 94.4%), and there is no logo.

**Visuals**

Vertical stacked bar chart titled '2021e content budgets ($bn)'. Y-axis: linear, $bn, 0 to 35 with gridlines and labels every 5 (0, 5, 10, 15, 20, 25, 30, 35), labels in grey #545554; the x-axis baseline is a thin grey line at 0. Dotted light-grey horizontal gridlines at each 5-unit step. X-axis categories left to right: Disney, Netflix, Comcast, Amazon, ViacomCBS, Warner, Discovery, Fox, Apple, UK, FR, DE, ES, IT combined. Series: Film & TV (dark grey #535353, bottom segment) and Sport (mid grey #A6A6A6, top segment). Netflix, Amazon and Apple bars are filled red #EB483F as single-colour bars (no split shown); the UK, FR, DE, ES, IT combined bar is light grey #DDDDDD. Printed values: none are printed on the bars; estimates from gridlines: Disney Film & TV about 23.0 and Sport about 8.6 (total about 31.7); Netflix about 15.8 (red); Comcast Film & TV about 15.2 and Sport about 7.0 (total about 22.2); Amazon about 13.0 (red); ViacomCBS Film & TV about 12.0 and Sport about 2.5 (total about 14.5); Warner Film & TV about 11.6 and Sport about 1.7 (total about 13.3); Discovery Film & TV about 5.0, no Sport; Fox Film & TV about 3.0 and Sport about 4.7 (total about 7.7); Apple about 1.3 (red); UK, FR, DE, ES, IT combined about 12.0 (light grey). Legend at upper left of the chart: grey square #A6A6A6 labelled Sport above dark grey square #535353 labelled Film & TV. Highlighted points: the red-filled bars for Netflix, Amazon and Apple.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide, with a white #FFFFFF band behind the chart area. Flat, minimal consulting-style look with no shadows, gradients or rounded corners. Background light grey #F1F1F1 in the title and footer bands with white #FFFFFF in the content band. Title text in dark grey #545454 in a high-contrast serif (Photina-style, regular weight, roughly 5.6% of slide height, about 0.7 cap height relation as a title). Subtitle in the same serif, regular, about 2.6% of slide height, colour #545454. Chart title and body labels in a neo-grotesque sans (Helvetica Neue style): chart title bold about 2.2% of slide height in #545554, axis and category labels regular about 1.85% in #535353 or #545554. Footer text in the same sans, regular, about 1.85%, source in #545454 and author name bold #A6A6A6 with date and page number regular #A6A6A6. Bars have no outlines; gridlines are thin dotted grey; accent colour red #EB483F used only for three bars.

### Slide 57

**Text**

Big fish become small fish / For UK 16-34s, streaming from US companies is now bigger than total viewing for the entire UK TV industry / UK 16-34s daily video viewing (minutes) / YouTube / 300 / Subscription streaming / Broadcaster VOD / Recorded / 250 / Live TV / 200 / 150 / 100 / 50 / 0 / 2017 / 2018 / 2019 / 2020 / Source: Ofcom / Benedict Evans –– January 2022 / 57

**Layout**

Full-slide light grey (#F1F1F1) field with a white chart panel spanning the middle band between y 20.5% and y 86.1%. The title sits top-left at x 20.1%, with a one-line subtitle directly beneath it, both left-aligned on the same x. A chart title sits at x 20.5%, y 27.4%. A vertical legend sits at the far left (x 3.2% to 16%) beside a stacked column chart occupying x 20.6% to 95.4%, y 36% to 80%. Category years sit below the baseline. The footer band carries 'Source: Ofcom' at left (x 20.5%) and the author, date and slide number at right, ending at x 95.3%. No slide-number box is in a corner; the number 57 sits at the right end of the footer line.

**Visuals**

Chart: stacked column chart, title 'UK 16-34s daily video viewing (minutes)'. Y axis: minutes per day, linear, 0 to 300, gridlines and labels every 50 (0, 50, 100, 150, 200, 250, 300), light grey dotted gridlines. X axis: years 2017, 2018, 2019, 2020, solid mid-grey baseline at 0. Four stacked columns, each about 7.4% of slide width, bottom to top: Live TV (#535353), Recorded (#A6A6A6), Broadcaster VOD (#DCDCDC), Subscription streaming (#EB483F), YouTube (#F8DBD8). Segment values are not printed on the slide; the values below are estimates marked 'about'. 2017: Live TV about 96, Recorded about 24, Broadcaster VOD about 10, Subscription streaming about 30, YouTube about 59, total about 219. 2018: Live TV about 82, Recorded about 22, Broadcaster VOD about 10, Subscription streaming about 52, YouTube about 64, total about 230. 2019: Live TV about 67, Recorded about 24, Broadcaster VOD about 11, Subscription streaming about 65, YouTube about 67, total about 234. 2020: Live TV about 66, Recorded about 25, Broadcaster VOD about 11, Subscription streaming about 91, YouTube about 72, total about 265. Legend, top to bottom: YouTube (#F8DBD8 swatch), Subscription streaming (#EB483F swatch), Broadcaster VOD (#DCDCDC swatch), Recorded (#A6A6A6 swatch), Live TV (#535353 swatch); swatches are small squares about 1.6% high. Segments are separated by thin white hairline borders. No highlighted points. No photographs, logos or icons.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide, with a white (#FFFFFF) panel behind the chart from y 20.5% to 86.1%. Light grey #F1F1F1 background with a white chart panel; a clean editorial look. Title in a light serif (Photina-style) in dark grey #545454, regular weight, large. Subtitle in the same serif, regular, dark grey #545454, smaller. Chart title and footer author name in bold sans-serif (Helvetica Neue Bold) #545554 and #A6A6A6. Axis labels, legend and source in Helvetica Neue regular, #535353 or #545554. Title cap height is about 5.6% of slide height; body and axis text about 1.85% of slide height. Accent colours: red #EB483F, pale pink #F8DBD8, greys #535353, #A6A6A6, #DCDCDC. Square-cornered shapes, no shadows, no gradients. Gridlines are light grey dotted hairlines.

### Slide 58

**Text**

New control points?
Apple announced the Apple TV and iPhone at the same event in 2007…
But TV software was a backwater for a decade
Now the TV has become a battleground, powered by the shift from live to streaming
And the target is $200bn of legacy TV advertising
Source: Sky
Benedict Evans
–– January 2022
58

**Layout**

Two-column split: the left half (0 to 50% width) is a light grey panel holding the title and four body paragraphs stacked top-down with left alignment at x 4.6%; the right half is white holding a centred device image. The source line sits bottom-left at x 4.6%, y 90.9%; the footer 'Benedict Evans –– January 2022' and slide number 58 sit bottom-right of the white half on the same baseline at y 91.1%, with the slide number at the right margin near x 94.4%.

**Visuals**

Photograph/illustration, one image at x 50.7%, y 17.0%, w 49.3%, h 68.2% (982 by 737 pixels), placed on the white right half without a box. It shows a flat-screen television viewed from the front, seen in a frontal, horizontally centred composition. The TV screen is dark navy blue with a glowing blue jellyfish swimming diagonally from upper-left toward lower-right, trailing a stream of bright blue sparkling particles; the screen fills most of the frame. Below the screen is a solid dark blue bottom bezel band (#2B4A7A approx.) and a narrow dark blue central stand foot. Behind and above the screen top edge, four slim panels fan out like cards: a pale pink panel on the left (#F9E4E4 approx.), a dark green panel with a sparkling texture (#1E6B3E approx.), a silver-white panel (#E6E6E6 approx.), and a black panel at far right (#1A1A1A approx.). Lighting is soft and even, the image is not cropped, and there is no overlay or tint.

**Design**

Background is light grey #F2F2F2 on the left half and white #FFFFFF on the right half. Title and body text are dark grey #545454; footer and slide number are light grey #A6A6A6; source line is dark grey #545454. Title uses PhotinaMT, a transitional serif, regular weight, at about 5.56% of slide height (cap height about 3.9% of height). Body uses PhotinaMT regular at about 2.96% of slide height, line spacing about 1.4 times the size. Footer and source use HelveticaNeue sans-serif; 'Benedict Evans' is HelveticaNeue-Bold, rest is HelveticaNeue regular, all at about 1.85% of slide height. No shapes, lines, borders, shadows or gradients are present.

**Elements and positions** (back to front; x, y, width, height as % of the slide; type, colour and alignment in brackets)

### Slide 59

**Text**

Welcome to 2007 / YouTube launched in 2005, and Hulu in 2007 / Deploying old ideas that are finally ready to work / Source: Techcrunch / Benedict Evans –– January 2022 / 59

**Layout**

Two-column split: a light grey left panel (left half) carries the title, two body lines and the source line, while the right half is white and holds a single framed browser screenshot centred vertically. Title sits top-left at about 24% of slide height, body lines are left-aligned to x 4.6%, source line sits bottom-left and the footer (author, date, slide number) sits bottom-right of the white half.

**Visuals**

One photograph-style screenshot of a web page in a browser window, boxed with a soft drop shadow, not cropped. Browser chrome at top: three small traffic-light dots (red, amber, green) at left, a rounded address bar in the centre reading 'techcrunch.com' with a lock icon, a reload icon and a page icon. Page content: green 'TC' logo at top-left; green hamburger menu icon at top-right; a round white close button with a green X overlapping the top-right corner of the page; bold black headline 'Clown Co. Gets Website and New Name: Hulu'; grey metadata line '/ 6:57 PM GMT+1 • August 29, 2007' with a green comment icon at right; a grid of about 20 small TV show and video thumbnails in five columns and four rows, with a central white panel reading 'hulu' in large grey type, a small grey line 'Sign up for an invite to the Hulu private beta, coming in October', an input field and 'Submit' button and a green 'Find out more' link; body text in black with green hyperlinks 'dubbed “Clown Co.”' and 'Hulu'; a green social share bar overlapping the lower edge of the page with facebook, twitter, linkedin, email and share icons on green squares. Lighting is flat and even; dominant colours are white, black and green (#00C853-like).

**Design**

Background: Split background: #F2F2F2 on the left half and #FFFFFF on the right half. Light grey #F2F2F2 left panel and pure white #FFFFFF right panel. Title in a light serif (PhotinaMT) in dark grey #545454, body in the same serif in #545454. Source line and footer in Helvetica Neue regular in #545454 and #A6A6A6; author name in Helvetica Neue Bold #A6A6A6. Title approximately 5.6% of slide height, body approximately 3.0%, footer approximately 1.9%. Screenshot has a soft grey drop shadow and no border radius on the window; the address bar is rounded.

**Text**

Retail unbundles / Lockdown has pulled forward future adoption - but much less in the USA than elsewhere / Ecommerce as a share of addressable retail* / UK (monthly) / USA (quarterly) / 40% / 30% / 20% / 10% / 0% / January 2010 / January 2012 / January 2014 / January 2016 / January 2018 / January 2020 / January 2022 / Source: ONS, US Census / * Excluding cars, car parts, fuel, bars & restaurants. Seasonally adjusted / Benedict Evans / –– January 2022 / 60

**Layout**

Two-band layout on a full-bleed grey field: a light grey top band holds the title and subtitle, a white middle band holds a single full-width line chart with its title above the plot, and a light grey footer band holds the source lines at left and the author, date and slide number at right. Title and subtitle are left-aligned at x 20.1% with a common left edge; the chart title, y-axis labels and source line share the x 20.5% edge. The legend sits in the left margin at x 3.2% to 11.5%, outside the plot column. Slide number is at the bottom right, x 94.4%.

**Visuals**

Chart: line chart, titled 'Ecommerce as a share of addressable retail*'. Y axis: percentage from 0% to 40%, gridlines every 10% (dotted light grey), labels 0%, 10%, 20%, 30%, 40%. X axis: time from January 2010 to January 2022, labelled every two years (January 2010, 2012, 2014, 2016, 2018, 2020, 2022), solid grey baseline at 0%. Series 1, 'UK (monthly)', red line: starts about 6.5% in January 2010, about 8% in 2011, about 10% in 2013, about 13% in 2015, about 16% in 2017, about 19.5% in late 2019, rises sharply to about 31% in early 2020, peaks about 34% in mid 2020, dips to about 29% in late 2020, peaks about 36.5% in early 2021, falls to about 28% by late 2021 and ends about 28% at January 2022. Series 2, 'USA (quarterly)', dark grey line: starts about 6% in January 2010, about 10% in 2015, about 16.8% in 2019, peaks about 22% in Q2 2020, falls to about 20.5% in late 2020, about 19.7% in early 2021 and about 20% by late 2021, ending at about 20%. Legend: short red line segment labelled 'UK (monthly)' above short dark grey segment labelled 'USA (quarterly)', both at top left of chart. No highlighted points.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 across the full slide, with a white #FFFFFF band from 20.5% to 86.1% of the height. Flat, minimal style with no shadows, gradients or ornaments. Background band #F1F1F1 top and bottom, white #FFFFFF middle band. Title in a light serif (Photina-like) regular weight, colour #545454. Subtitle same serif, #545454. Chart title in Helvetica Neue Bold, #545454. Chart and footer text in Helvetica Neue regular, #545454 or #535353. Footer author name in Helvetica Neue Bold and date and page number in Helvetica Neue regular, #A6A6A6. Accent colours: UK line red #E8443A, USA line dark grey #4A4A4A. Gridlines dotted #B0B0B0 hairlines; x-axis baseline solid #8C8C8C 1px. Title size about 5.6% of slide height, subtitle about 2.6%, body and chart labels about 1.85%, footer about 1.8%.

### Slide 61

**Text**

(The future is unevenly distributed) / Over 80% of people in Britain buy online regularly, but less than a third of Italians / Percentage of population buying online in last 3 months, Europe / 2020 / 2019 / 2015 / 100% / 75% / 50% / 25% / 0% / UK / DK / DE / NL / SE / IRL / FR / BE / FI / AT / ES / CZ / PL / HU / EL / PT / IT / TR / RO / Source: Eurostat. Data for France in 2020 not available / Benedict Evans / –– January 2022 / 61

**Layout**

Full-width header band in light grey with the title left-aligned at about 20% of slide width and the subtitle directly beneath it; a white content band fills the middle (about 20.5% to 86% of height) holding the chart title, legend at far left and a dot plot across the full chart width. The footer sits on the grey base band with the source line aligned to the chart's left edge and the author, date and slide number right-aligned near the right margin.

**Visuals**

One dot plot (scatter-style) titled 'Percentage of population buying online in last 3 months, Europe'. Y axis: percentage of population, 0% to 100% in 25% steps, with light dotted horizontal gridlines at 25%, 50%, 75% and 100%; no axis title. X axis: 20 European country codes in order UK, DK, DE, NL, SE, IRL, FR, BE, FI, AT, ES, CZ, PL, HU, EL, PT, IT, TR, RO, sorted by descending 2020 value; solid grey baseline at 0%. Three series, each one filled circle per country: 2020 red dots, 2019 dark grey dots, 2015 light grey dots. Legend at far left, top-left of chart, stacked vertically: red dot 2020, dark grey dot 2019, light grey dot 2015. No values are printed; approximate values read from positions (about): UK 2020 about 83%, 2019 about 80%, 2015 about 75%; DK 2020 about 79%, 2019 about 74%, 2015 about 67%; DE 2020 about 75%, 2019 about 71%, 2015 about 64%; NL 2020 about 77%, 2019 about 70%, 2015 about 59%; SE 2020 about 73%, 2019 about 70%, 2015 about 56%; IRL 2020 about 64%, 2019 about 59%, 2015 about 44%; FR 2020 not available, 2019 about 58%, 2015 about 49%; BE 2020 about 65%, 2019 about 55%, 2015 about 42%; FI 2020 about 60%, 2019 about 55%, 2015 about 49%; AT 2020 about 56%, 2019 about 54%, 2015 about 46%; ES 2020 about 54%, 2019 about 47%, 2015 about 32%; CZ 2020 about 59%, 2019 about 43%, 2015 about 26%; PL 2020 about 47%, 2019 about 41%, 2015 about 24%; HU 2020 about 49%, 2019 about 35%, 2015 about 23%; EL 2020 about 38%, 2019 about 32%, 2015 about 24%; PT 2020 about 35%, 2019 about 28%, 2015 about 23%; IT 2020 about 31%, 2019 about 28%, 2015 about 18%; TR 2020 about 24%, 2019 about 23%, 2015 about 11%; RO 2020 about 22%, 2019 about 15%, 2015 about 8%. No highlighted points; the 2020 series is drawn on top of the others, and 2019 dots partly overlap 2020 dots in UK, DK, DE, NL, SE, AT, TR. No photographs, logos or icons.

**Design**

Background: Light grey #F1F1F1 full-slide background with a white #FFFFFF band from 20.5% to 86.1% of slide height. Flat, minimal design: light grey header and footer bands (#F1F1F1) around a pure white chart band. Title and subtitle in a light serif (Photina-style, regular weight, not bold) in dark grey #545454. Chart title and axis labels in a Helvetica Neue-style neo-grotesque sans-serif; chart title bold, axis labels and legend regular, in dark grey #545454 to #535353. Accent colour is red #E94B3C for the 2020 series. Title is about 5.6% of slide height; subtitle about 2.6%; chart title about 2.2%; axis labels, legend and footer about 1.9%. Gridlines are thin dotted light grey, baseline is thin solid grey. No shadows, gradients, corner radii or rounded panels.

62

**Layout**

Full-bleed light grey (#F1F1F1) slide with a white content band across the middle. The title and subtitle sit in the top grey band, left-aligned at about 20% of slide width, in one left-aligned column. The chart fills the white band from about 20% to 94% of slide width, with a legend in the left margin at about 5% x. The footer sits in the bottom grey band: source lines left-aligned at about 20.5% x, and the author, date and page number right-aligned in the same band. No slide number other than the printed 62 in the footer.

**Visuals**

Chart: line chart titled 'UK ecommerce as % retail*'. Y axis is percentage share of retail from 0% to 70%, gridlines every 10%. X axis is time from 2010 to September 2021, labelled at 2010, 2015, 2020 and September 2021. Three series: 'Share of non-grocery' in dark grey (about #545454), 'Share of total' in red (about #E8473F), and 'Share of grocery' in light salmon pink (about #F4A09A). No values are printed on the chart. Estimated values: non-grocery starts about 10% in 2010, rises to about 19% by 2015, about 30% by early 2020, peaks about 59% in spring 2020, then falls to about 42% by the end, ending near 41%. Total starts about 6% in 2010, about 15% in 2015, about 19% in early 2020, peaks about 35% in 2020, and ends about 28%. Grocery starts about 2% in 2010, about 4% in 2015, about 5% in early 2020, peaks about 12% in late 2020, and ends about 11%. Legend at left: three short line swatches with labels in the same colours as the series, stacked vertically, top to bottom: Share of non-grocery, Share of total, Share of grocery. No points are highlighted. Gridlines are thin dotted light grey horizontal lines across the plot. The X axis baseline is a thin solid grey line at 0%.

**Design**

Background light grey #F1F1F1 for top and bottom bands, white #FFFFFF for the central chart band. Title in PhotinaMT-style serif, regular weight, dark grey #545454, about 5.6% of slide height. Subtitle in the same serif, regular, #545454, about 2.6% of slide height. Chart subtitle in HelveticaNeue-Bold sans-serif, #545554, about 2.2% of slide height. Chart labels, legend and footer source in HelveticaNeue sans-serif regular, #545454 or #535353, about 1.8% to 1.9% of slide height. Author and date in HelveticaNeue with Bold author name, colour #A6A6A6, about 1.85% of slide height. Lines are 2 to 3 px solid strokes with no shadows, gradients or rounded corners. Gridlines are thin dotted #BFBFBF-style lines.

**Layout**

Full-width header band across the top fifth of the slide with the title and subtitle left-aligned at about x 20 percent, a white content band below holding a single stacked column chart with its title above the plot, and a grey footer band holding the source line at left and the author, date and page number at right. Title and subtitle sit inside the header; the page number is at the bottom right at about x 94 percent.

**Visuals**

One vertical stacked column chart. Title: 'US retail revenue by delivery model, 2020 ($bn)'. Y axis: 0 to 2,500 in $bn, labelled every 500 with light dotted horizontal gridlines at 500, 1,000, 1,500, 2,000 and 2,500. X axis: three categories, 'Could be parcels', 'Needs delivery or collection' and 'Restaurants*', with a solid grey baseline at 0. Series as printed: 'Could be parcels' column is about 2,130 total, with Ecommerce about 670 in red at the bottom and Physical retail about 1,460 in dark grey on top (no value printed on the segments). 'Needs delivery or collection' column is about 1,050 total, with a thin red base about 80, Grocery about 780 in dark grey, and Other about 190 in dark grey on top. 'Restaurants*' column is about 765 total, with a thin red base about 55, Analogue off-prem about 250 in pink, and On-prem about 460 in dark grey on top. Segments are separated by thin white lines. No legend; series are labelled inside segments in white text. No highlighted points. Footnote in source line: cars, car repair and gasoline not shown; asterisk means 2019.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 across the full slide, with a white #FFFFFF band behind the chart area. Flat, minimal consulting-style look. Header and footer bands are light grey #F1F1F1 with the content area white #FFFFFF. Title is a regular serif (Photina MT or similar Didone-free serif), dark grey #545454, at about 5.6 percent of slide height. Subtitle is the same serif in #545454 at about 2.6 percent. Chart title and axis labels are Helvetica Neue, the chart title bold, in #545454 at about 1.9 to 2.2 percent. Chart colours: dark grey #535353, red #EB483F, light pink #F5A39F. Segment labels are white Helvetica Neue regular at about 1.85 percent. Footer uses Helvetica Neue in #A6A6A6, author name bold. Gridlines are thin dotted grey. No shadows, gradients or rounded corners.

**Layout**

Full-bleed light grey (#F1F1F1) slide with a grey header band across the top and a grey footer band below a white content panel (y 20.5% to 86.1%). The title sits top-left at x 20.1%, aligned left with the subtitle beneath it and the chart title. The chart fills the white panel from x about 4.8% (legend) to about 95% (right edge of the 2020 bar area). Source line sits bottom-left at x 20.5%, and the author, date and page number sit bottom-right, ending at x about 95.6%.

**Visuals**

Chart: stacked vertical bar chart, titled Annual US parcel volume (bn). Y-axis: parcel volume in billions, 0 to 25, gridlines every 5 (dotted light grey). X-axis: years 2014 to 2020, one category per year. Series stacked bottom to top: USPS (dark grey #535353), UPS (mid grey, about #808080), Fedex (light grey, about #A6A6A6), Amazon Logistics (red, about #EE4B3B). Estimated values, read from bar heights because the slide prints no values: 2014 USPS about 3.4, UPS about 3.6 (top about 7.0), Fedex about 2.1 (top about 9.1), Amazon none. 2015 USPS about 4.0, UPS about 3.7 (top about 7.6), Fedex about 2.2 (top about 9.8), Amazon none. 2016 USPS about 4.4, UPS about 3.8 (top about 8.2), Fedex about 2.3 (top about 10.5), Amazon about 0.1 (top about 10.7). 2017 USPS about 5.0, UPS about 4.0 (top about 9.0), Fedex about 2.4 (top about 11.4), Amazon about 0.4 (top about 11.8). 2018 USPS about 5.6, UPS about 4.2 (top about 9.8), Fedex about 2.5 (top about 12.3), Amazon about 0.7 (top about 13.0). 2019 USPS about 5.6, UPS about 4.4 (top about 10.0), Fedex about 2.7 (top about 12.7), Amazon about 1.8 (top about 14.5). 2020 USPS about 7.6, UPS about 4.9 (top about 12.5), Fedex about 3.2 (top about 15.7), Amazon about 4.2 (top about 19.9). Legend: vertical list at upper left, top to bottom Amazon Logistics (red square), Fedex (light grey square), UPS (mid grey square), USPS (dark grey square); no highlighted points. Bars are flat with thin white separators between segments and no outlines; baseline at 0 is a thin grey line.

**Design**

Background: Light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide, with a white #FFFFFF band behind the chart area.

65

**Layout**

Full-width slide split into a light grey header band across the top (about 20% of height) with the title and subtitle left-aligned at about x 20%, and a white body area below holding a single line chart whose plot runs from about x 20.6% to 95.3%. The chart title sits above the plot at x 20.5%; the legend is in the left margin at x 4.8%; a light grey footer band holds the source line at x 20.5% and the author, date and page number on the right, with the page number at about x 94.4%.

**Visuals**

Line chart titled 'Retail revenue ($bn)'. Y axis: linear, labelled 0, 100, 200, 300, 400, 500, 600, with light dotted horizontal gridlines at each label; axis unit $bn. X axis: years labelled 1980, 1990, 2000, 2010, 2020, with the final point labelled 'Sept 2021 TTM'; data spans 1980 to about 2021. Series 1, Walmart net sales: solid dark grey (#545454) line about 3 px wide, starting near 0 in 1980, about 35 in 1990, about 180 in 2000, about 400 in 2008, about 470 in 2013, about 480 in 2016, about 510 in 2018, about 555 in 2020, ending about 567 in 2021. Series 2, Amazon ex. AWS: solid red (#E8493F) line about 3 px wide, starting in 1995 at 0, about 25 in 2009, about 100 in 2015, about 200 in 2018, about 335 in 2020, ending about 400 in 2021. Series 3, Amazon GMV (e): red dotted line, starting about 2006 near 15, about 25 in 2009, about 100 in 2013, about 210 in 2016, about 320 in 2018, about 550 in 2020, ending about 583 in 2021. Legend: three entries at top-left of plot, each with a short line swatch in its series style (dark grey solid, red solid, red dotted). No highlighted points, no annotations, no logos, no photographs.

**Design**

Background: Light grey #F1F1F1 full-bleed, with a white #FFFFFF panel from y 20.5% to 86% behind the chart. Light grey (#F1F1F1) full-slide background with a white body panel behind the chart. Title in PhotinaMT serif regular, dark grey #545454, about 5.6% of slide height. Subtitle in PhotinaMT serif regular, #545454, about 2.6% of height. Chart title and axis labels in HelveticaNeue sans-serif, #545454, about 1.9% of height; chart title in HelveticaNeue Bold. Footer source in HelveticaNeue #545454; author name in HelveticaNeue Bold #A6A6A6 and date and page number in HelveticaNeue #A6A6A6, about 1.9% of height. Accent colour red #E8493F for Amazon series; dark grey #545454 for Walmart. Lines are thin, gridlines are light grey dotted. No shadows or gradients, no corner radii.

### Slide 66

**Text**

New channels have new toll booths / ‘Merchant media’ - Amazon has >$25bn of ad revenue, possibly producing more cash than AWS / Amazon ‘other, predominantly ads’ revenue, TTM ($bn) / 40 / 30 / 20 / 10 / 0 / Mar-17 / Sep-17 / Mar-18 / Sep-18 / Mar-19 / Sep-19 / Mar-20 / Sep-20 / Mar-21 / Sep-21 / Source: Amazon / Benedict Evans –– January 2022 66

**Layout**

Full-bleed light grey (#F1F1F1) slide with a white band across the middle; a grey header band holds the title and subtitle left-aligned at about x 20.1% with a 5.5% top margin. The chart sits in the white band, its title left-aligned at x 20.5% and the plot spanning about x 20% to 94%, with y-axis labels at the left and x-axis labels beneath. A grey footer band holds the source line at left (x 20.5%) and the author, date and page number right-aligned toward x 95%.

**Visuals**

One line chart. Type: line chart with circular markers. Title: ‘Amazon ‘other, predominantly ads’ revenue, TTM ($bn)’. Y-axis: $bn, range 0 to 40, labelled 0, 10, 20, 30, 40, with dotted light grey horizontal gridlines at each labelled value. X-axis: time, labelled Mar-17 to Sep-21 at six-month intervals, with a thin grey baseline at 0. Series: one series, red line (about #EE3F35) about 3pt thick with red markers about 1.2% of slide height in diameter, 19 quarterly points from Mar-17 to Sep-21. Values are not printed on the slide; estimates are about: Mar-17 3.3, Jun-17 3.6, Sep-17 4.0, Dec-17 4.7, Mar-18 5.9, Jun-18 7.1, Sep-18 8.5, Dec-18 10.1, Mar-19 10.8, Jun-19 11.6, Sep-19 12.7, Dec-19 14.1, Mar-20 15.3, Jun-20 16.5, Sep-20 18.2, Dec-20 21.4, Mar-21 24.4, Jun-21 28.2, Sep-21 30.8. Legend: none. Highlighted points: none; the final point is the last marker at the right edge. The chart image is a 1441 by 479 pixel placed image with no border.

**Design**

Background light grey #F1F1F1 for header and footer bands, pure white #FFFFFF for the middle band. Title and subtitle in a serif (PhotinaMT, close to a Photina/Times-like serif, regular weight) in grey #545454; title about 5.6% of slide height, subtitle about 2.6%. Chart title in Helvetica Neue Bold, #545554, about 2.2%. Axis labels in Helvetica Neue regular, #545554, about 1.85%. Source line in Helvetica Neue regular #545454 about 1.85%. Footer author name in Helvetica Neue Bold #A6A6A6, date and page number in Helvetica Neue regular #A6A6A6, all about 1.85%. Accent colour is the red series #EE3F35. Lines are thin dotted grey gridlines and a solid grey baseline. No corner radii, shadows or gradients.

### Slide 67

**Text**

Endless waves of new competitors / Shopify powered over $160bn of e-commerce in the last 12 months / Shopify GMV ($bn) / 175 / 150 / 125 / 100 / 75 / 50 / 25 / 0 / 2014 / 2015 / 2016 / 2017 / 2018 / 2019 / 2020 / Sept 2021 TTM / Source: Shopify / Benedict Evans –– January 2022 67

**Layout**

Two-tier slide: a light grey header band (top 0 to 20.5% of height) holds a left-aligned serif title and subtitle starting at x 20.1%. A white content band (20.5% to 86.1%) holds a single column chart with its chart title left-aligned at x 20.5% and a plot area spanning roughly x 18% to 96%. A grey footer band (86.1% to 100%) holds the left-aligned source line at x 20.5% and the right-aligned author, date and page number ending at x 95.6%, all on one baseline near y 91%.

**Visuals**

One column chart titled 'Shopify GMV ($bn)'. Y axis: numeric, unitless labels 0 to 175 in steps of 25, with light dotted horizontal gridlines at each step; no axis title. X axis: categories 2014, 2015, 2016, 2017, 2018, 2019, 2020, 'Sept 2021 TTM' with a thin solid grey baseline at 0. Series: single series of Shopify GMV in $bn, values not printed on the slide; estimated from bar heights: 2014 about 3, 2015 about 7, 2016 about 15, 2017 about 25, 2018 about 40, 2019 about 60, 2020 about 119, Sept 2021 TTM about 162. Bars are dark grey #535353 except the final 'Sept 2021 TTM' bar, which is highlighted in red #EB483F. No legend. No data labels. Highlighted point: the Sept 2021 TTM bar, the tallest bar on the chart.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 with a white band #FFFFFF across the middle of the slide. Light grey background #F1F1F1 for header and footer bands with a white content band between them. Titles and subtitle in a serif face (Photina, regular weight) in grey #545454. Chart title in Helvetica Neue Bold, grey #545554. Axis labels and source line in Helvetica Neue regular, grey #545454 and #545554. Footer author name in Helvetica Neue Bold and date and page number in Helvetica Neue regular, light grey #A6A6A6. Title is about 5.6% of slide height, subtitle about 2.6%, chart title and axis labels about 1.9% to 2.2%. Accent colour is red #EB483F used only on the highlighted bar. Bars are flat with square corners and no shadows or gradients. Gridlines are thin dotted grey lines.

**Layout**

Full-bleed light grey (#F1F1F1) slide with a grey header band across the top holding the title and subtitle, left-aligned at about x 20%, and a white chart band from about y 20.5% to 86% spanning the full width. The chart sits in the white band with a legend at the far left (x about 4.8%) and the plot area from about x 18% to 95%; the source line sits at bottom left (x 20.5%) and the footer author, date and page number sit at bottom right in the grey footer band, with the page number at about x 94.4%.

**Visuals**

Grouped vertical bar chart titled 'Ecommerce GMV ($bn)' (bold). Y-axis: 0 to 400, gridlines every 100 as fine dotted grey lines, unit $bn implied by chart title. X-axis: categories 2014, 2015, 2016, 2017, 2018, 2019, 2020, Sept 2021 TTM. Three series per category, left to right: Amazon 1P (dark grey #535353), Amazon Marketplace (e) (mid grey #A7A7A7), Shopify (red #EC4A3C). The slide prints no values, so estimates are marked 'about': 2014 about 68 / 66 / 4; 2015 about 77 / 80 / 8; 2016 about 91 / 107 / 15; 2017 about 108 / 137 / 27; 2018 about 122 / 170 / 42; 2019 about 140 / 211 / 61; 2020 about 197 / 321 / 119; Sept 2021 TTM about 222 / 360 / 161. Legend at top left of the chart with square swatches in the same colours, stacked vertically: Amazon 1P, Amazon Marketplace (e), Shopify. No highlighted points; bars have thin white separations and no shadows.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide, with a white (#FFFFFF) band behind the chart from y 20.5% to 86.1%. Minimal flat style. Header and footer bands #F1F1F1 with a white chart band in between. Title in a light serif (Photina-like) in dark grey #545454, large. Subtitle in the same serif, #545454, smaller. Chart title and axis labels in a neutral Helvetica Neue sans-serif, #545454 to #535353; chart title bold. Footer author name bold in #A6A6A6 Helvetica Neue, date and page number regular #A6A6A6. Source line Helvetica Neue regular #545454. Title size about 5.6% of slide height, subtitle about 2.6%, body and axis text about 1.9%. Gridlines dotted light grey, baseline solid grey. No corner radii, gradients or shadows.

### Slide 69

**Text**

New gatekeepers / “We personalise ads; you track your users; they violate / fundamental human rights” / Smartphones are not a neutral platform… / And this has a big impact on D2C brands / Conflict between privacy and competition regulators / Source: Apple / Ad / Personalized Ads / Personalized ads in Apple apps such as the App Store and Apple News help you / discover apps, products, and services that are relevant to you. We protect your / privacy by using device-generated identifiers and not linking advertising / information to your Apple ID. / Turning on Personalized Ads increases the relevance of ads shown by letting us / use data like account information, app and content purchases, and where / available, the types of News stories you read. / Apple does not track you or share your personal information with any third parties. / Learn More / Turn On Personalized Ads / Turn Off Personalized Ads / Benedict Evans — January 2022 / 69

**Layout**

Two-column split slide: a light grey left panel (left half) holds the title, four body statements and the source line, and a white right half holds a single framed UI screenshot centred in the panel. The title sits top-left of the left column at about 24% from the top, with the statements stacked below at left margin 4.6%. The source line sits bottom-left at about 91% from the top, and the footer (author, date, slide number 69) runs along the bottom of the right half at about 91% from the top, with the slide number at the far right.

**Visuals**

One photograph-like UI screenshot of an iOS-style settings screen, shown as a white rectangular card with a soft grey drop shadow beneath and to the right, boxed and not cropped, centred in the right half. The card shows a blue rounded-rectangle badge reading Ad in white at the top centre, a bold black heading Personalized Ads, three centred paragraphs of small black sans-serif text, a blue Learn More link, a large blue rounded button labelled Turn On Personalized Ads in white, and below it a blue text link Turn Off Personalized Ads. Lighting is flat and even, with no tint or overlay; dominant colours are white, black text and bright blue #3478F6. No logos or icons beyond the Ad badge.

**Design**

Background: Split background: left half solid light grey #F2F2F2, right half solid white #FFFFFF. Two flat background panels: left panel light grey #F2F2F2 and right panel white #FFFFFF, meeting at the vertical midline with no border. Titles and body statements are dark grey #545454 in a classical serif (PhotinaMT, regular weight, lowercase, sentence case), title about 5.6% of slide height and body statements about 3.0%. Footer and source text are in Helvetica Neue sans-serif, about 1.9% of slide height; the author name is bold in light grey #A6A6A6 and the date and slide number are regular light grey #A6A6A6, source line is #545454. Accent colour is the screenshot blue #3478F6. The screenshot card has square corners on the card itself, a large soft shadow, and blue rounded corners on the badge and button.

### Slide 71

**Text**

Brands go digital / B2B businesses learning new channels / Ecommerce as % sales, 2021 / 30% / 25% / 20% / 15% / 10% / 5% / 0% / L'Oreal / Nestlé / P&G / Unilever / Colgate / Kellogg's / Source: Companies / Benedict Evans — January 2022 / 71

**Layout**

A full-width grey (#F1F1F1) header band holds the title and subtitle, left-aligned at x 20.1%, with the title's top at about 5.5% of the height. A white band fills the middle from y 20.5% to 86%, containing a single bar chart whose plot area runs from about x 17.5% to 95%. A grey footer band below holds the source line at left (x 20.5%) and the author, date and page number at right, aligned on one baseline near y 91%. No grid or column structure beyond the chart's six category slots; the slide number sits at the right margin, x 94.4%.

**Visuals**

One vertical bar chart. Title: 'Ecommerce as % sales, 2021', set above the plot in bold sans-serif. Y axis: percentage of sales, 0% to 30%, labelled every 5% (0%, 5%, 10%, 15%, 20%, 25%, 30%), with thin dotted light-grey horizontal gridlines at each 5% step. X axis: six companies, with a solid grey baseline at 0%. Series, one series of six bars. Values are not printed on the slide, so these are estimates: L'Oreal about 28% (highlighted in red), Nestlé about 15%, P&G about 14%, Unilever about 11%, Colgate about 10%, Kellogg's about 9%. Highlighted point: the L'Oreal bar is the only red bar (#EB483F); all other bars are dark grey (#535353). Legend: none. Bars are flat-filled rectangles, about 5% of slide width each, evenly spaced. No logos, photographs or icons.

**Design**

Background: Light grey #F1F1F1 full-bleed, with a white #FFFFFF band from y 20.5% to 86%. Flat, minimal look. Backgrounds are light grey #F1F1F1 for the header and footer bands and white #FFFFFF for the chart band. Title and subtitle are set in a book-weight serif (PhotinaMT-like, Photina family), title in #545454 at about 5.6% of slide height and subtitle in #545454 at about 2.6%. Chart title is bold Helvetica Neue-style sans-serif at about 2.2% in #545554. Axis and category labels are regular Helvetica Neue at about 1.85% in #545554. Source line is regular Helvetica Neue at 1.85% in #545454. Footer author name is bold Helvetica Neue in #A6A6A6, date and page number regular in #A6A6A6, all about 1.85%. Accent colour is red #EB483F used only on the L'Oreal bar. Bars #535353 with no outlines, no shadows, no gradients, no rounded corners. Gridlines are dotted light grey, about 0.1% stroke. Baseline is a thin solid grey line.

### Slide 72

**Text**

New experiences break in / Shein breaks in to fast fashion with a new UX and new data models (and a massive acquisition budget) / Worldwide search interest / Zara / H&M / Shein / 100 / 75 / 50 / 25 / 0 / 2005 / 2010 / 2015 / 2020 / Source: Google Trends / Benedict Evans — January 2022 / 72

**Layout**

Full-width horizontal band layout: a light grey header band across the top (about 0 to 20.5% of height) holds the title and subtitle, left-aligned at about x 20.1%. A white content area fills the middle (about 20.5 to 86.1% of height). A light grey footer band (about 86.1 to 100%) holds the source line at left (x 20.5%) and author, date and page number at right, ending at about x 95%. The chart sits in the white area from about x 18% to 95%, with a legend in a narrow column at the far left (x 4.8%). Margins are roughly 20% left for the header and chart, with the page number flush to the right edge of the chart area.

**Visuals**

Chart: a line chart titled Worldwide search interest (bold, placed above the plot at about x 20.5%, y 27.4%). Type: time-series line chart with three series, no fill under lines. X axis: calendar years from 2005 to about 2022, with labelled ticks at 2005, 2010, 2015 and 2020; monthly data points, thin baseline at 0. Y axis: search interest index from 0 to 100, labelled ticks at 0, 25, 50, 75 and 100; light dotted horizontal gridlines at each labelled tick. No units printed. Series: Zara, dark grey (about #555555), line about 2 px wide, starts about 13 in 2005, rises to about 25 by 2008, about 50 to 60 in 2011 to 2013, about 65 to 75 in 2015 to 2017, peaks at 100 around late 2017 (about 100), fluctuates between about 60 and 90 through 2019 to 2021, dips to about 50 in early 2020, ends about 79 in early 2022. H&M, light grey (about #AAAAAA), starts about 12 in 2005, about 20 to 30 in 2008 to 2010, about 35 to 50 in 2011 to 2015, peaks about 57 around 2019, and settles about 33 to 40 through 2020 to 2022, ending about 37. Shein, red (about #E8473B), no data before about 2015.5, about 1 in 2016, about 3 in 2017, about 7 in 2018, about 14 in 2019, rising to a peak about 52 in mid 2020, dropping to about 34 then rising to about 60 to 65 by late 2021, ending about 63. Legend: three entries at top left of chart: Zara with dark grey line swatch, H&M with light grey line swatch, Shein with red line swatch, labels in regular sans-serif. No highlighted points. Line chart has no markers. Dates labelled only at 5-year intervals.

**Design**

Background: flat light grey #F1F1F1 for the header and footer bands, with a white #FFFFFF central content band. Title colour #545454 (dark grey), subtitle colour #545454. Body and chart label text #545454. Chart title in bold #545554. Footer author name bold #A6A6A6 and date and page number #A6A6A6. Accent: the only saturated colour is the red Shein line (about #E8473B). Typefaces: title and subtitle in PhotinaMT, a serif with regular weight; chart title, axis labels, legend, source, and footer in HelveticaNeue (regular, bold for chart title and author name), a neo-grotesque sans-serif. Title text is about 5.6% of slide height, subtitle about 2.6%, body and chart labels about 1.85%. Lines: chart series lines about 2 px thick; gridlines are thin dotted light grey; the baseline at 0 is a thin solid grey rule. No shapes with corner radii, no shadows, no gradients.

**Layout**

A full-width light grey (#F1F1F1) header band holds the left-aligned serif title and subtitle, with the title starting at about 20% of slide width. A white panel fills the middle band below it, containing a chart title and a legend at the upper left, and a single stacked column chart spanning from about 17% to 95% of slide width. A grey footer band sits below the white panel, with the source line left-aligned at about 20.5% and the author, date and page number right-aligned in the footer at about 75% to 96% of slide width.

**Visuals**

Chart: stacked column chart titled 'Female US teenagers' top 10 favourite online shopping brands'. Y-axis is percentage of total, 0% to 100%, labelled at 0%, 25%, 50%, 75%, 100%, with light dotted horizontal gridlines at each labelled value. X-axis categories are Spring 2020, Autumn 2020, Spring 2021 and Autumn 2021, with a thin grey baseline at 0%. Four stacked columns, each about 7.4% of slide width, with thin white separators between segments. Bottom segment is Amazon in #535353, middle segment is Shein in #EB483F, top segment is Next 8 in #7F7F7F. No printed data labels; the values below are estimates. Spring 2020: Amazon about 43%, Shein about 5% (about 43% to 48%), Next 8 about 26% (about 48% to 74%), total about 74%. Autumn 2020: Amazon about 44%, Shein about 11% (about 44% to 55%), Next 8 about 22% (about 55% to 77%), total about 77%. Spring 2021: Amazon about 47%, Shein about 14% (about 47% to 61%), Next 8 about 21% (about 61% to 82%), total about 82%. Autumn 2021: Amazon about 39%, Shein about 18% (about 39% to 57%), Next 8 about 24% (about 57% to 81%), total about 81%. Legend at upper left of the chart, stacked top to bottom: grey square #7F7F7F labelled Next 8, red square #EB483F labelled Shein, dark square #535353 labelled Amazon. No highlighted points.

**Design**

Background is light grey #F1F1F1 for the header and footer bands, with a white #FFFFFF panel for the chart area. Title is set in a serif (PhotinaMT-style, Times-like) regular weight in dark grey #545454, about 5.6% of slide height. Subtitle is the same serif in regular weight, #545454, about 2.6% of slide height. Chart title and axis labels are in a Helvetica Neue-style sans-serif; chart title is bold #545554 at about 2.2% of slide height; axis and legend labels are regular #535353 or #545554 at about 1.85% of slide height. Source line is regular sans-serif #545454 at about 1.85%. Footer author name is bold sans-serif #A6A6A6 and date and page number are regular #A6A6A6 at about 1.85%. Accent colours are red #EB483F for Shein and dark grey #535353 for Amazon, with mid grey #7F7F7F for Next 8. Shapes are flat with square corners, no shadows, no gradients.

**Layout**

Two bands frame the slide: a light grey header band across the top holding the title and subtitle, and a light grey footer band at the bottom holding the source line and the author, date and page number. The white middle band holds a single left-aligned chart with its title above the plot, a legend at the far left, and a horizontal x-axis. Title and subtitle start at about x 20.1%, the source line at x 20.5%, and the footer right block ends near x 95.6% with the page number at the right margin.

**Visuals**

Chart: stacked column chart titled 'US fast fashion market share'. Vertical axis is percentage share, 0% to 100%, gridlines at 25% intervals (0%, 25%, 50%, 75%, 100%), dotted light grey. Horizontal axis shows four categories: January 2020, July 2020, January 2021, July 2021. Each column is 100% stacked from bottom to top: Shein (red), H&M (dark grey), Zara (mid grey), Others (light grey). Jan 2020: Shein about 7%, H&M about 26% (top at about 33%), Zara about 10% (top about 43%), Others about 57% (to 100%). Jul 2020: Shein about 19%, H&M about 21% (top about 40%), Zara about 5% (top about 45%), Others about 55% (to 100%). Jan 2021: Shein about 13%, H&M about 23% (top about 36%), Zara about 9% (top about 45%), Others about 55% (to 100%). Jul 2021: Shein about 28%, H&M about 20% (top about 48%), Zara about 11% (top about 59%), Others about 41% (to 100%). Legend at far left, top to bottom: Others (light grey square), Zara (mid grey square), H&M (dark grey square), Shein (red square). Segments are separated by thin white lines. The Shein segments are the only highlighted colour (red #EB483F) against greys.

**Design**

Background is light grey #F1F1F1 for the header and footer bands, with a white #FFFFFF band between them. Title is a regular-weight serif (PhotinaMT-like, Photina/Times-like) in dark grey #545454, about 5.6% of slide height. Subtitle is the same serif in #545454, about 2.6% of height. Chart title and axis labels are a sans-serif (Helvetica Neue) about 1.9% of height; chart title is bold #545554. Body and axis text is Helvetica Neue regular #545454. Accent colour is red #EB483F for the Shein series. Series colours: Others #A6A6A6, Zara #808080, H&M #535353, Shein #EB483F. Columns are flat rectangles with no corner radius, no shadows, no gradients; gridlines are thin dotted light grey; the horizontal baseline is a thin grey line. Footer author name is bold #A6A6A6, date and page number regular #A6A6A6.

### Slide 75

**Text**

This has all happened before
Department stores were Amazon, once - this is another generational change
Share of US retail sales
Department stores
Superstores
Ecommerce
17.5%
15.0%
12.5%
10.0%
7.5%
5.0%
2.5%
0.0%
1995
2000
2005
2010
2015
2020
Source: BLS
Benedict Evans
–– January 2022
75

**Layout**

Full-bleed light grey (#F1F1F1) slide with a grey header band across the top holding the title and subtitle, left-aligned at about 20% of slide width. A white content panel spans the full width between about 20.5% and 86% of slide height, holding the chart title, legend at the far left and a line chart filling the panel. A grey footer band sits below, with the source line at left and the author, date and slide number right-aligned at the bottom. Margins are about 20% left for the title and chart area and about 5% at the far left for the legend.

**Visuals**

Line chart titled Share of US retail sales. Y-axis: percentage of US retail sales, range 0.0% to 17.5%, gridlines every 2.5% drawn as light grey dotted horizontal lines. X-axis: years 1995 to 2021, labelled every five years (1995, 2000, 2005, 2010, 2015, 2020), with a solid mid-grey baseline at 0.0%. Legend at upper left of chart: grey line for Department stores, dark charcoal line for Superstores, red line for Ecommerce. Series Department stores (light grey #A6A6A6 line, about 2 pt): about 9.3% in 1995, roughly flat to 1996, declining to about 7.8% in 2000, about 6.5% in 2004, about 5.0% in 2009, about 4.6% in 2011, about 3.6% in 2015, about 2.8% in 2018, about 2.4% in 2020, dipping to about 1.9% in 2020-21 and ending about 2.1%. Series Superstores (charcoal #4A4A4A line, about 2 pt): about 2.8% in 1995, about 4.4% in 2000, about 6.5% in 2004, about 8.0% in 2007, a peak of about 10.0% around 2009, about 9.7% in 2010, about 9.4% in 2013, about 9.3% in 2018, a spike to about 10.1% around 2020, ending about 8.8%. Series Ecommerce (red #E8473B line, about 2.5 pt): starts about 0.6% in 2000, about 1.1% in 2001, about 3.7% in 2009, about 4.6% in 2011, about 6.6% in 2015, about 9.5% in 2018, about 11.4% in 2020, a sharp peak of about 15.7% around mid-2020, then falling to about 13.9%, 13.6% and ending about 13.4% in 2021. No data labels are printed; all values above are estimates. No highlighted points are marked.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 across the whole slide, with a white #FFFFFF panel covering the middle band. Light grey #F1F1F1 background for header and footer bands, white #FFFFFF content panel. Title in PhotinaMT-style serif, regular weight, dark grey #545454, about 5.6% of slide height. Subtitle in same serif, regular weight, #545454, about 2.6% of slide height. Chart title and axis labels in Helvetica Neue, bold for chart title (#545554) and regular for tick labels (#545554), about 1.9% to 2.2% of slide height. Legend labels Helvetica Neue regular #535353. Source and footer in Helvetica Neue regular #545454 and #A6A6A6, author name in Helvetica Neue bold #A6A6A6. Lines are thin with no shadows, corner radii or gradients.

### Slide 76

**Text**

Is this about good tech? / Or good retailing?
Benedict Evans
–– January 2022
76

**Layout**

Two-column split with a light grey left panel holding a left-aligned two-line title vertically near the middle of the slide, and a full-height photograph filling the right half. The title sits left of centre with a left margin of about 4.6% of the slide width. The footer line (author, date, page number) runs along the bottom at about 91% of the slide height, over the photograph at the right, with the page number at the far right edge.

**Visuals**

One photograph on the right half of the slide: a street-level view of a Topshop storefront on a sunlit city street. Subject: a pale stone building facade with two cream-globed brass wall lanterns, a large upper window showing a faint mannequin behind glass, and at ground level a dark fascia with the letters TOPSHOP in outlined white/grey lettering inside a glazed sign panel. Below are glass entrance doors with mannequins visible inside, a window at left showing a green-tinted display, and metal crowd-control barriers along the pavement; one barrier carries a small multicolour site-safety notice headed 'Site Safety' with pictograms and red, blue and yellow panels. Yellow painted lines run across the pavement, with a kerb and dark road beyond. Composition: eye-level, straight-on, slightly off-centre, the building fills the frame edge to edge and is cropped at the left and right edges and at the bottom by the road. Lighting: bright direct sunlight from the upper left casting strong dark shadows of the lanterns and the building on the stone. Dominant colours: beige and grey stone, black fascia, blue sky reflections in windows, yellow pavement lines, white-cream lantern globes. The photo is full-bleed on the right half with no border, frame or overlay.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F2F2F2 on the left half, with a white #FFFFFF right half behind the full-bleed photograph. Two flat fills: a light grey #F2F2F2 background across the whole slide, and a white #FFFFFF panel on the right half that is covered by the photograph. The title is set in a classical serif (PhotinaMT, close to a Photina/Times-like text serif), regular weight, in dark grey #545454, two lines, at about 5.6% of slide height per line with generous line spacing. The footer uses Helvetica Neue in light grey #A6A6A6; the author name is bold and the date and page number are regular, all about 1.9% of slide height. No shadows, gradients, rules or corner radii.

**Elements and positions** (back to front; x, y, width, height as % of the slide; type, colour and alignment in brackets)

- shape at x 0, y 0, w 100, h 100 [#F2F2F2]: Full-slide background rectangle (Flat fill, no border, no shadow.)
- shape at x 50, y 0, w 50, h 100 [#FFFFFF]: Right-half panel rectangle (Flat fill, no border; fully covered by the photograph.)
- photo at x 50, y 0, w 50, h 100 [#C9BFAE]: Photograph of the Topshop storefront with TOPSHOP sign, lanterns, barriers and site-safety notice on a sunlit street (Full-bleed, unboxed, no overlay or tint. The placed image box is x 17.6, y 0, w 82.4, h 100 and is cropped so that only the right half is visible on the slide. Edges cropped at slide boundaries.)
- title at x 4.6, y 24.2, w 31, h 12 [PhotinaMT, regular weight, serif; font size 5.56% of slide height; #545454; left aligned]: Is this about good tech? / Or good retailing? ### Slide 77

**Text**

Questions from 1995? / Or questions from 1900? / Benedict Evans –– January 2022 / 77

**Layout**

Two-column split: a text column occupies the left half on a light grey field, with the two-line question set left-aligned at a left margin of about 4.6% and sitting roughly in the vertical middle of the slide (top line at 24.2% height). The right half is a full-bleed black-and-white photograph that runs off the top, right and bottom edges. A small footer row sits along the bottom at about 91% height, with the author name, date and page number 77 on one line; the page number is at the far right, near the photo edge.

**Visuals**

One photograph, full-bleed in the right half (x 32.6% to 100%, full height, cropped by the slide edges and overlapping the white right-half panel). Subject: a large early-20th-century classical department-store facade seen at an oblique angle, with a row of tall fluted Corinthian/Ionic columns between windows, ornamental stone carvings and cartouches along the cornice, and a row of flags on the roofline. Street level shows a crowd of pedestrians on the pavement, vintage double-decker buses with destination boards, a lorry and cars on the road, and a street lamp standard in the middle distance. Framing: wide, shot from street level at an angle toward the left, with the building filling the left and middle of the frame and the sky occupying the upper right. Lighting: flat, even overcast daylight with no strong shadows. Colours: monochrome greys and blacks only. No overlay or tint; the photograph is not boxed.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F2F2F2 on the left half, with a white #FFFFFF panel on the right half behind the full-bleed photograph. Flat, minimal look. Background light neutral grey #F2F2F2 for the left text area. Title text is set in a light serif (PhotinaMT regular) in dark grey #545454, large in relation to the slide, with no bold or italics. Footer text is set in Helvetica Neue in light grey #A6A6A6, very small; the author name is bold and the date and page number are regular. No accent colour, no lines, no shapes beyond the grey background and white right panel, and no shadows, gradients or rounded corners. The photograph sits on a white right-half panel #FFFFFF that is hidden behind it.

### Slide 79

**Text**

How do you serve your customers?
US brands & retailers spend $7-800bn to reach their customers - rent is the new CAC
Logistics
“How do I get it?”
Retail rents
$300bn+
Shipping
$130bn
Marketing
$200bn
Advertising
$200bn
Discovery
“What do I want?”
Source: Pitney Bowes, Zenith, Costar, US Census
Benedict Evans — January 2022
79

**Layout**

Single-slide composition on a full-bleed light grey field with a left-aligned title and subtitle starting at about 20% of slide width and near the top margin (title top at about 5.5% of height). A three-part horizontal row sits in the middle band: a red circle at left, a vertical stacked bar at centre, and a red circle at right, with grey arrows between them. The source line sits bottom-left at about 20.5% x and 91% y; the footer (author, date, page number) sits bottom-right aligned to a right margin near 95.6% of width. No slide-level logo.

**Visuals**

Two flat red filled circles, each with a centred two-line label in white serif text. Between them, a vertical stacked bar chart of four segments, bottom to top: Advertising $200bn (red #EB483F), Marketing $200bn (pale pink #FBDAD9), Shipping $130bn (dark grey #7F7F7F), Retail rents $300bn+ (mid grey #A6A6A6). Segments are separated by thin light outlines and form one column with no axis, gridlines or scale; the total is not printed. A thin grey baseline (#BFBFBF, about 0.1% of height) runs beneath the bar, wider than the bar. Two solid grey (#A7A7A7) block arrows: the left one points right from the Logistics circle toward the bar, the right one points left from the Discovery circle toward the bar. Each arrow has a triangular head and a rectangular shaft. No highlighted points or legend.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide. Light grey background (#F1F1F1) with no gradient or texture. Title in a light-weight high-contrast serif (PhotinaMT-style, regular weight) in dark grey #545454, large relative to the slide (about 5.6% of height). Subtitle in the same serif in #545454 at about 2.6% of height. Bar and circle labels in Helvetica Neue regular, white (#FFFFFF), about 1.85% of height; circle labels in the serif at about 3.3% of height. Accent colour is red #EB483F with a pale pink #FBDAD9 and greys #A6A6A6, #A7A7A7 and #7F7F7F. Footer text in Helvetica Neue, #A6A6A6, the author name bold. Shapes are flat with no corner radii (bar segments are rectangles), no shadows and no gradients. Circles are perfect circles.

Conclusion

The title 'Conclusion' sits top-left inside the panel, left-aligned at x 20.2%, y 27.1%. A thin white horizontal rule runs along the bottom edge of the panel. The footer sits bottom-right below the panel: author name in bold, em dash, date, then slide number at the far right. The panel body below the title is empty.

none

Background flat slate #36494E. Panel is white at 4% opacity over the background, giving a slightly lighter slate (about #3E5056). Footer in HelveticaNeue sans-serif, about 1.85% of slide height; author name in HelveticaNeue Bold, date and page number in HelveticaNeue regular, all grey #A6A6A6. Thin white rule about 0.3% of slide height, square ends, no shadow, no corner radius. Panel has square corners and no shadow.

### Slide 81

**Text**

All the things I left out
When software eats the world, everything becomes a software question
Privacy
Advertising
Frontier AI
Local
delivery
China
Moral
panics
Regulation
Semi-
conductors
Remote work
Splinternets
Security
M&A
Benedict Evans
—
January 2022
81

**Layout**

Left-aligned title block at the top, with the title and subtitle starting at about x 20% and y 5.5%. A loose cluster of circles fills the middle band from about y 23% to 86%, spanning x 20% to 94%, with no grid. The footer sits at the bottom right at about y 91%: author name in bold, an em dash, the date, then the page number at x 94.4%. The left side below the title is empty.

**Visuals**

Bubble cluster of about 27 circles, all filled with the same salmon-pink, with white outlines. Most are semi-transparent (opacity 0.50); the two large circles labelled China and Regulation are solid red #EB483F and are the highlighted points. Large circles carry labels: Privacy, Advertising, Frontier AI, Local delivery, Moral panics, Semi-conductors, Remote work, Splinternets, Security, M&A, plus the two red circles China and Regulation. Unlabelled small circles are decorative bubbles of two sizes, about 2.2% and 3.8% of slide width. No chart axes, legend, photograph or logo.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide. Flat, minimal look on a pale grey background. The title is a serif (PhotinaMT-like, roman) in dark grey #545454, around 5.6% of slide height. The subtitle is the same serif in #545454 at about 2.6%. Circle labels are white serif roman at about 3.0% (China and Regulation about 3.3%). Footer is a sans-serif (Helvetica Neue) in light grey #A6A6A6 at about 1.9%, with the author name bold. Circles use a white outline of about 0.3% of slide height, flat fills with no shadows or gradients.

### Slide 82

**Text**

Past, future, present
While tech builds the future, industries in its wake try to adjust
Tech dreams / for 2030
Building tech / startups today
Old economy / adjusts to tech / disruption
Benedict Evans — January 2022 82

**Layout**

Single-slide layout with the title and subtitle left-aligned at the top, starting at about 20% from the left edge, with three equal circles in a row across the middle-lower area of the slide, evenly spaced. The footer sits bottom right with the author name, date and page number on one baseline, right of centre, with a right margin of about 5%.

**Visuals**

Three filled circles of equal size (each about 22.1% of slide width by 39.3% of slide height, rendered as circles). Left circle: dark grey #535353 with white serif text 'Tech dreams / for 2030' centred. Middle circle: dark grey #535353 with white serif text 'Building tech / startups today' centred. Right circle: red #EB483F with white serif text 'Old economy / adjusts to tech / disruption' centred. No photographs, charts, logos or icons. Circles are flat with no shadow, outline or gradient.

**Design**

Background light grey #F1F1F1, flat. Title and subtitle in grey #545454 serif (PhotinaMT-style serif, regular weight), title about 5.6% of slide height, subtitle about 2.6%. Circle labels white #FFFFFF serif regular, about 3.3% of slide height, centred over two or three lines. Accent red #EB483F on the right circle only. Footer in light grey #A6A6A6: author name in bold Helvetica Neue sans-serif, date and page number in regular Helvetica Neue at about 1.85% of slide height. Shapes are plain circles with no corner radii, shadows or gradients.

### Slide 83

**Text**

The fall and rise of music / Music was one of the first industries to be overturned by the internet - how’s it doing? / Global recorded music revenue ($bn, 2020 dollars) / Streaming / Downloads / Synch / Performance / Physical / 40 / 30 / 20 / 10 / 0 / 2000 / 2005 / 2010 / 2015 / 2020 / Source: IFPI, BLS / Benedict Evans / –– January 2022 / 83

**Layout**

Full-width horizontal bands: a light grey header band across the top holding a left-aligned title and subtitle starting at x 20.1%, a white content band from y 20.5% to 86.1% holding the chart, and a light grey footer band below. The chart title sits at x 20.5%, y 27.4%. A legend sits at the far left (x 3.1% to 11%) beside the plot. The source line is at the bottom left of the plot column (x 20.5%, y 90.9%). The author credit, date and slide number sit bottom right, with the slide number at x 94.4%, y 91.1%, ending at the right margin of about 98.8%. Margins are about 20% on the left for the content column and about 5% on the right.

**Visuals**

Stacked area chart titled 'Global recorded music revenue ($bn, 2020 dollars)'. Y axis: $bn, 0 to 40, labelled 0, 10, 20, 30, 40, with faint dotted horizontal gridlines at 10, 20, 30 and 40. X axis: years 2000 to 2020, labelled every five years (2000, 2005, 2010, 2015, 2020). Five stacked series, bottom to top: Physical (dark grey #424242), Performance (mid grey #676768), Synch (light grey #A7A7A7), Downloads (pale pink #F8DBD8), Streaming (red #EB473F). No values are printed on the chart; approximate totals are about 34.5 in 2000, about 33 in 2001, about 31 in 2002, about 20 in 2009, about 15.5 in 2014, about 16.5 in 2016, about 19 in 2018 and about 21.5 in 2020. Physical is about 34.5 in 2000, about 30 in 2002, about 17 in 2007, about 12 in 2009, about 6.5 in 2014 and about 4.3 in 2020. Streaming is absent until about 2007, is a thin band to about 2013, and is about 13 in 2020 (about 8.2 to 21.5). Downloads is about 8 in 2014 and about 1 in 2020. Synch and Performance are thin bands of about 0.5 to 2 each. Legend at top left, listed top to bottom: Streaming (red), Downloads (pale pink), Synch (light grey), Performance (mid grey), Physical (dark grey), each with a small square swatch. The Streaming band is the only highlighted series (red against neutral greys and pale pink). No highlighted points.

**Design**

Background light grey #F1F1F1 in the header and footer bands, white #FFFFFF in the content band. Title colour #545454 in a serif (PhotinaMT-like, a light transitional serif) at regular weight, very large. Subtitle in the same serif, #545454, regular weight. Chart title and axis numerals in sans-serif (Helvetica Neue style). Chart title is bold, #545554. Legend and axis labels are regular sans-serif, #535353 to #545554. Source line is regular sans-serif #545454. Author name is bold sans-serif #A6A6A6, date and slide number regular sans-serif #A6A6A6. Title text is about 5.6% of slide height, subtitle about 2.6%, chart title about 2.2%, all other text about 1.85%. Series colours: red #EB473F, pale pink #F8DBD8, light grey #A7A7A7, mid grey #676768, dark grey #424242. Area boundaries are separated by thin white lines. Gridlines are thin dotted grey. No shadows, gradients or corner radii.

### Slide 84

**Text**

The scale of platform rents
Apple makes more from platform rents than the entire global music industry, or Netflix
Annual revenue* ($bn)

payments

Global recorded music

84
*2020 for music, September 2021 TTM for others

**Layout**

Full-width header band on a light grey field with the title flush left at about 20% of slide width, subtitle directly beneath it, then a white content panel holding a single bar chart with the chart title above the plot, and a grey footer band holding the source lines at left and the author, date and slide number at right. Slide number 84 sits at far right of the footer, aligned to the same baseline as the date.

**Visuals**

Vertical stacked and simple bar chart titled 'Annual revenue* ($bn)' (bold, left-aligned above the plot). Y axis is linear from 0 to 40 in steps of 10, labelled 40, 30, 20, 10, 0 at left of the plot, with light dotted horizontal gridlines at each labelled step and a solid thin grey baseline at 0. X axis has three categories labelled Global recorded music, Netflix, Apple platform rent. Series: Global recorded music bar, dark grey #535353, value about 21.5. Netflix bar, dark grey #535353, value about 28.5. Apple platform rent stacked bar with bottom segment App Store commissions in red #EB483F, value about 17, and top segment Google TAC payments in light salmon #F5A39F, value about 12, total about 29. Labels are printed inside the Apple segments in white; no printed values for the other bars, so values are estimates. No legend. No highlighted points beyond the red and salmon colouring of the Apple bar. Bars are flat with no shadows and no outlines; the segment boundary is a thin white line.

**Design**

Background light grey #F1F1F1 for header and footer bands with a white #FFFFFF content panel between them. Title in a light serif (PhotinaMT-like, regular weight) in grey #545454, about 5.6% of slide height, roughly 0.7 cap height. Subtitle in the same serif, regular, grey #545454, about 2.6% of slide height. Chart title and chart labels in Helvetica Neue, bold for chart title and regular for others, dark grey #545554, about 1.9% to 2.2% of slide height. Footer text Helvetica Neue regular #545454 for source, author in Helvetica Neue bold #A6A6A6 and date and page number in regular #A6A6A6, about 1.9% of slide height. Accents are red #EB483F and salmon #F5A39F. Shapes are square-cornered with no shadows or gradients. Dotted gridlines are light grey about #BFBFBF, 0.5 px dotted.

### Slide 87

**Text**

Moving to bigger opportunities
Tech has gone from selling accounting tools to big companies to replacing those companies
Music: $21bn / Books: $90bn
Advertising & / marketing: / $1tr+
Retail: $20tr+
Consumer / spending: / $50tr
Source: IFPI, PwC, World Bank
Benedict Evans — January 2022
87

**Layout**

Single-column content on a light grey field with the title and subtitle left-aligned at about x 20 percent, sharing the same left edge. A horizontal row of four equal circles with grey arrows between them sits across the vertical middle of the slide, starting at x 19.4 percent and ending at about 96 percent. The source line sits bottom-left at x 20.5 percent, and the author, date and slide number sit bottom-right at y 91 percent, with the slide number at x 94.4 percent.

**Visuals**

Process diagram of four circles connected left to right by three block arrows. Circle 1 is dark grey #535353 with the labels 'Music: $21bn' and 'Books: $90bn' on two lines in white. Arrow 1 is a solid light grey #A6A6A6 right-pointing block arrow with a rectangular shaft and triangular head, between circle 1 and circle 2. Circle 2 is dark grey #535353 with 'Advertising & / marketing: / $1tr+' in white, centred. Arrow 2 is identical to arrow 1. Circle 3 is dark grey #535353 with 'Retail: $20tr+' in white, centred. Arrow 3 is identical to arrow 1. Circle 4 is red #EB483F, the only highlighted element, with 'Consumer / spending: / $50tr' in white, centred. All four circles are the same size, about 16.2 percent of slide width and 28.8 percent of slide height, and sit on one horizontal line with equal gaps. No chart axes, legend or photographs.

**Design**

Background light grey #F1F1F1, flat with no gradient. Title in a serif typeface resembling Photina (a transitional serif), regular weight, colour #545454, about 5.6 percent of slide height. Subtitle in the same serif, regular weight, #545454, about 2.6 percent of slide height. Circle labels in the same serif, regular weight, white #FFFFFF, about 3.0 percent of slide height. Footer text in a Helvetica Neue-style sans-serif, regular weight, about 1.85 percent of slide height; the author name is bold #A6A6A6, the date and slide number are regular #A6A6A6, the source line is regular #545454. Circles are flat fills with no outlines, shadows or gradients. Arrows are flat #A6A6A6 block shapes with no outlines.
